## 2026-09-21 — `M6-STT-FE-129`: confirmation is a backend hold with a real gate, not a client-side stub over an eager answer

**Decision:** Below `Settings.stt_confirmation_confidence_threshold` (default `0.6`), `askwell.voice_stt.build_stt_driver` stores nothing and calls no `on_transcript` hook until a client resolves a new server-side confirmation future — via `confirm` (proceed with the transcript as heard) or `edit` (proceed with a replacement) control messages `askwell.voice_channel._receive_loop` now understands. A turn that gets neither within `Settings.stt_confirmation_timeout_seconds` (default 120s) ends unanswered, with nothing written to `messages`/`audit_interactions` at all.

**Why:** Issue #468, filed while scoping this ticket, found that `build_stt_driver` called `on_transcript` unconditionally right after storing a transcript — there was no pause point between "transcript stored" and "generation begins," and no message type for a client to submit a correction. Two ways to close that gap were on the table. The rejected one: keep generation eager, and if a client-submitted edit arrives before the spoken answer finishes, generate a second time from it — cheaper to build, but the user hears or reads an answer to the wrong question at least once even when they do edit, which is exactly the "confident answer to a question I did not ask" this ticket exists to prevent (its own User Story, almost verbatim). The alternative, taken here, holds the turn before generation ever starts, so an unconfirmed low-confidence transcript is never answered at all. That also settles a question `AGENTS.md` §3 C6 raises but doesn't answer on its own — "the confirmed or edited transcript is what is recorded" reads two ways (store the raw transcript immediately and correct it later, or store nothing until confirmed) — resolved here as the latter: storing an unconfirmed guess and silently overwriting it if the user chooses to edit would mean a wrong transcript briefly existed in an append-only-in-spirit audit trail for no reason grounded in anything real.

The timeout (rather than holding indefinitely) exists because a WebSocket driver task deliberately outlives its connection for reconnection support (`voice_channel`'s own module docstring) — without a timeout, a user who closes the tab mid-confirmation would leave a driver task parked forever, waiting on a future nothing will ever resolve.

**Consequences:** A confidence-threshold change is a configuration change, not a code change — same shape as `retrieval_score_threshold` and `voice_vad_pause_ms` already are, both still unmeasured starting points for the same reason this one is (no transcribed-speech or retrieval-relevance corpus exists in this environment yet). If a future ticket wants a client-initiated "cancel" distinct from a silent timeout, it has to add a new control message rather than reuse `edit`/`confirm` — neither is safe to send with a false or empty payload, by design (`_receive_loop`'s blank-edit guard).

**Refs:** issue #468 (resolved, closed); `docs/ux/voice.md` §5 "Low confidence"; `docs/states-and-edge-cases.md` §5; `api/src/askwell/voice_channel.py` (`VoiceTurn.request_confirmation`/`resolve_confirmation`, `_receive_loop`); `api/src/askwell/voice_stt.py` (`build_stt_driver`); `api/src/askwell/config.py` (`stt_confirmation_confidence_threshold`, `stt_confirmation_timeout_seconds`); `web/lib/voice.ts`, `web/components/ask/voice-control.tsx` (the `confirming` state); issue #460 (re-owned, unchanged scope).

---

## 2026-09-21 — `M6-VUI-FE-135`: permission read proactively via the Permissions API, non-English handled as a real channel fact, and a naturally-completed answer stays shown rather than only a stopped one

**Decision:** Three independent fixes, all in `web/lib/voice.ts` and `web/components/ask/voice-control.tsx`, none touching the backend.

1. **Permission denial is read once, up front**, from `navigator.permissions.query({name: "microphone"})` on mount, via a new pure `micPermissionReason(state)` — never a proactive `getUserMedia` call, since that API *prompts*, and the Permissions API only *reports*. When it reports `"denied"`, `startCapture` refuses to call `getUserMedia` at all until `PermissionStatus.onchange` says otherwise; a browser without Permissions API support for `"microphone"` falls back to exactly the pre-existing behaviour (the real denial from a real `getUserMedia` attempt sets the same reason).
2. **`nextVoiceStatus` gained a `language` case.** The channel has sent this event since `M6-STT-BE-127`, typed in `VoiceChannelEvent` since `M6-VUI-FE-128a`, but the state machine never read it — an unsupported-language turn previously just sat in whatever state it was interrupted in until the closing `status` event reset it to plain idle with no explanation at all. It now ends the turn immediately with a stated English-only reason, and the following `status: "completed"` for the same turn is taught not to clobber that reason (`current.state === "idle" && current.reason !== null` short-circuits the reset) — the two events are guaranteed to arrive in that order (`askwell.voice_stt`'s own code path), so this is a real ordering fact, not a guess.
3. **`statusLabel`'s idle case now shows the answer whenever `reason` is `null`**, not only when `reason === VOICE_STOPPED_REASON`. Previously, any turn that finished normally — an abstention very much included — had its full text visible right up until the closing `status: "completed"` event landed, at which point the tooltip reverted to "Press and hold to speak" and the answer vanished. Nothing about this ticket's assigned scope required touching that logic, but it directly contradicts `M6-VUI-FE-135`'s own Acceptance Criterion ("an abstention is spoken completely and matches the screen word for word") — a screen that shows it and then un-shows it half a second later is not "shown in full."

**Why:** Rejected alternative for (1): keep asking on every idle press and let the browser silently no-op or re-prompt, as before. That satisfies "never disabled proactively" but not the ticket's own stated need ("an explanation and a path, not an error" *before* the user has to discover the block by pressing"), and repeatedly invoking `getUserMedia` against a denied permission is closer to "requesting permission repeatedly" than the ticket's Out of Scope line tolerates, even though most browsers reject instantly rather than re-prompting. Reading state instead of requesting it is strictly safer and costs one `Promise` on mount.

Rejected alternative for (3): special-case abstention specifically (e.g. a `VOICE_ABSTAINED_REASON` marker set from a citation-less answer, or a server-sent `abstained` flag over the voice channel). Rejected because the underlying bug is generic — *any* normally-completed voice answer disappeared, not just an abstention — and inventing an abstention-specific code path in the frontend to fix a general display bug would be exactly the kind of special-casing `AGENTS.md` warns against; fixing the general case makes the abstention case correct as a consequence, for free, and keeps voice mode's screen text identical to a typed answer's for every outcome, not just this one.

**Consequences:** A future ticket adding more idle-reason states must remember that `reason === null` now means "show the answer, if there is one" — a new state that sets a *non-null* reason while an answer already exists (there is no such case today) would need its own explicit precedence decision, the same way `VOICE_STOPPED_REASON` already gets one. Issue #460 (voice turns don't render into `AskProvider`'s conversation) is unaffected and still open — commented there noting this ticket also renders in place, not in the transcript.

**Refs:** `docs/ux/voice.md` §5, §6; `web/lib/voice.ts` (`micPermissionReason`, `VOICE_NON_ENGLISH_REASON`, `nextFromChannelEvent`); `web/components/ask/voice-control.tsx` (`statusLabel`, the Permissions API effect); `web/lib/voice.test.ts`; `api/src/askwell/voice_stt.py` (`unsupported_language`, unchanged); `api/src/askwell/agent/abstain.py` (`compose_abstention`, unchanged); issue #460 (re-owned, unchanged scope).

---

## 2026-09-21 — `M6-VUI-FE-133`: the stop control's own guards move into `web/lib/voice.ts` as pure predicates rather than staying inline in the component, and the post-stop label never claims "partial"

**Decision:** Two pure functions, `canStartCapture(status)` and `canStop(status)`, now govern (a) whether pressing-and-holding the mic may start a new turn — true only when `idle` — and (b) whether the stop control renders and does anything — true only while `answering`. `startCapture`'s existing early-return guard (`M6-VUI-FE-128a`, already correct, previously untested per issue #463) now calls `canStartCapture` instead of comparing `status.state !== "idle"` inline; a new `stop_pressed` action in `nextVoiceStatus` calls `canStop` the same way, transitioning `answering → idle` with a new `VOICE_STOPPED_REASON` ("Stopped.") and leaving every other state untouched — a second press, or one that lands after the turn already finished on its own, is inert because the predicate says so, not because of a separate re-entrancy flag. Pressing stop sends `{"type": "stop"}` over the socket (mirrored server-side onto `_Turn.stop_requested`, unchanged since `M6-TTS-BE-130`) and, locally, closes the playback `AudioContext` immediately to discard whatever audio is queued or already playing, rather than waiting for the server's own `status` event.

The post-stop label deliberately says only "Stopped." — never "partial" — even though the ticket's own Validation Rule states "a stopped answer is always marked partial." The `messages` row *is* marked correctly (`askwell.ask`'s existing `partial = status == "stopped" or truncated`, evaluated inside `_run_generation`'s own token loop, which already distinguishes "stop landed before the loop exited" from "stop landed after"), but the voice channel's own wire event (`VoiceTurn.status`, `"completed"`/`"failed"`) never forwards that distinction to a client — there is no event on `/voice/ws` that would let this control tell "stop cut the answer off" apart from "stop landed after the text was already complete and only cut off audio" (the ticket's own second edge case). Asserting "partial" here anyway would be a guess dressed as a fact.

**Why:** Issue #463, filed during this pickup, found that "speaking over the answer does nothing" — the ticket's own first Acceptance Criterion, and a pre-existing behaviour from `M6-VUI-FE-128a` — had no automated test, because the guard that implements it lives in `voice-control.tsx`, which touches `getUserMedia`/`WebSocket`/`AudioContext`, and `web/` has no `.test.tsx` component-test harness. Introducing one for a single assertion was rejected as disproportionate (the same trade-off `docs/decisions.md`'s `stopRequested` reasoning already made for the sibling half of this same component); lifting the guard into a pure predicate in `lib/voice.ts`, already `node:test`-covered without a browser, closes the gap for both the old guard and this ticket's new one with the same mechanism, at the cost of one small indirection.

For the label: the alternative was having the client *infer* whether generation had actually finished before stop landed — e.g. treating "no `text` event received in the last N ms" as a proxy for "generation is done, only synthesis is running." Rejected outright: it is exactly the kind of invented signal `AGENTS.md` §4 and C5's "abstention over invention" spirit rule out, would be wrong under ordinary network jitter, and — critically — the one place that actually knows the answer (`askwell.ask._run_generation`'s token loop) already writes the correct fact to `messages`/`audit_interactions` regardless of what this label says. Filed as issue #471 rather than closed silently, since a future ticket giving the voice channel a real `stopped`/`completed` distinction on the wire (needed anyway once issue #460 folds a voice turn into `AskProvider`'s own turn list, which already renders "Stopped. The answer above is partial." for the typed path) is a real, separate piece of work.

**Consequences:** Any future voice-channel event added to distinguish stopped-during-generation from stopped-during-synthesis-only should update `VOICE_STOPPED_REASON`'s usage in `voice-control.tsx` to say so precisely, and issue #471 tracks that. Until then, the authoritative partial marking lives only in `messages`/`audit_interactions` — correct, but not yet visible anywhere in the voice UI itself, which is the same gap issue #460 already names for the rest of the turn's content.

**Refs:** `docs/ux/voice.md` §4 #13, §5; `web/lib/voice.ts`; `web/components/ask/voice-control.tsx`; `web/lib/voice.test.ts`; `api/src/askwell/ask.py` (`partial = status == "stopped" or truncated`); `api/src/askwell/voice_tts.py` (`_speak_answer`'s `ask_turn.stop_requested = True`); issues #463 (closed by this ticket), #471 (filed by this ticket), #460 (re-owned, unchanged scope).

## 2026-09-21 — `M6-PERF-TEST-136`: per-stage timing marks live on `VoiceTurn` itself and travel as one `timing` event, rather than being reconstructed from trace data after the fact; the harness takes `--profile` from the operator, checked against a local hardware probe, rather than trusting the running server

**Decision:** `askwell.voice_channel.VoiceTurn` gained a `stage_timings` dict and a first-write-wins `mark(name)` method. Six checkpoints are marked across the voice path — `speech_ended` (`voice_channel._receive_loop`, on every path that closes `audio_in`), `transcription_done` (`voice_stt`, after `/transcribe` returns), `retrieval_started`/`generation_started` (`voice_tts._drain_events`, reading `askwell.ask`'s own `step` events rather than re-measuring retrieval independently), and `first_sentence_ready`/`first_audio_ready` (`voice_tts._speak_sentence`, around the first `/synthesize` call). `stage_breakdown_ms`, a pure function, turns these into five named millisecond intervals; `_speak_answer` emits them as one `timing` WebSocket event right before the turn's closing audio sentinel. The measurement harness (`eval/voice_latency.py`) reads this event directly — it never derives a stage duration from its own wall-clock reading of when a frame arrived.

The harness's `--profile` is a required operator-supplied argument (one of `light`/`standard`/`accelerated`/`workstation`), checked against `askwell.hardware.probe()` run on the harness's own machine, rather than read from `GET /health`.

**Why:** The alternative for timing was reconstructing a breakdown after the fact from `messages.trace` (which already carries `retrieve`/`compose` step durations, `M2-ABSTAIN-RET-053`) plus client-observed WebSocket frame arrival times. Rejected: `trace` is written once, at turn completion, by `askwell.ask._run_generation`, and voice reuses that function directly (`M6-TTS-BE-130`) — reading it back would mean a second DB round trip after the harness already has everything it needs from the turn it just ran, and it still would not cover transcription or synthesis, which have no `trace` step at all. Marking checkpoints on `VoiceTurn` itself, where every stage's driver already runs, costs one dict write per stage and needs nothing else to be true afterward. The four attributable intervals are constructed to sum exactly to the fifth (`first_audio_ms`, the ticket's own headline metric) so a miss is always attributable to one stage, never lost in an unaccounted gap — proven by `test_voice_timing.py`'s own sum assertion.

For `--profile`: `GET /health` reports `Settings.profile` (`light`/`balanced`/`full`, `askwell.config.Profile`) — a different enum from the hardware tier this ticket's budget table is keyed on (`light`/`standard`/`accelerated`/`workstation`, `docs/architecture.md` §6). `M6-VUI-FE-134` hit this same mismatch and reads `GET /setup`'s `probe.tier` instead of `/health`'s `profile` for its own latency indicator. This ticket goes one step further and calls `askwell.hardware.probe()` directly rather than adding a second HTTP round trip to `/setup` — valid under "one user, one machine" (`AGENTS.md` §1), since the harness always runs on the same machine as the stack it measures. What this does **not** solve is confirming the *server's own* configuration (which model it actually loaded) agrees with the tier the operator claims to be measuring — filed as issue #466 rather than guessed around here, since inventing a mapping between the two enums without a decision on which one is authoritative would be exactly the kind of guess `AGENTS.md` §4 forbids.

**Consequences:** Any future voice-path change that adds a new stage (streaming ASR partials, a second synthesis pass) needs a new mark and a new entry in `stage_breakdown_ms`'s `_STAGE_INTERVALS`, or it silently falls outside the breakdown rather than causing an error — a gap worth watching for, not fixed here since no such stage exists yet. The harness cannot be pointed at a server it has not verified matches the claimed profile without a person first bringing the stack up on that profile — by design, per the ticket's own "not measured" edge case, at the cost of an extra manual step compared to trusting `/health`. Issue #466 is a prerequisite for a future harness that wants to verify the server's configuration too, not just the local machine's hardware.

**Refs:** `AGENTS.md` §1, §4; `docs/build-plan.md` Phase 5; `docs/ux/voice.md` §1; `api/src/askwell/voice_channel.py`; `api/src/askwell/voice_stt.py`; `api/src/askwell/voice_tts.py`; `eval/voice_latency.py`; `api/tests/test_voice_timing.py`; `api/tests/test_voice_tts.py`; issue #466.

---

## 2026-09-21 — `M6-VUI-FE-128a`: push-to-talk over click-to-toggle, and a `ScriptProcessorNode` over an `AudioWorklet`, for the composer's mic capture

**Decision:** The composer's mic control (`web/components/ask/voice-control.tsx`) is push-to-talk — `pointerdown` starts capture, `pointerup`/`pointerleave`/`pointercancel` stops it and sends `{"type": "end"}` — rather than click-to-toggle with a separate stop action. Capture itself downsamples and encodes to 16 kHz mono PCM16 inside an `AudioContext.createScriptProcessor` callback, not an `AudioWorklet`.

**Why:** `docs/ux/voice.md` §7 had already settled push-to-talk as the only v1 mode, for hands-free's own reasons (a voice-activity threshold that misfires reads as the product interrupting itself). This ticket adds a second, independent reason to keep it: `askwell.voice_channel`'s turn detector (`M6-STT-BE-128`) closes a turn on its own once it hears a pause, entirely server-side, with no event telling the client it happened. A click-to-toggle control has no honest moment to enter "transcribing" — the only real signal available client-side of "capture has stopped" is the client's own release action. Push-to-talk turns that gap into a feature: releasing the button is a real local fact the reducer (`nextVoiceStatus` in `web/lib/voice.ts`) can transition on immediately, satisfying the ticket's own validation rule ("never optimistic") by construction rather than by inventing a heuristic for when the user has probably stopped talking. A click-to-toggle version was considered and rejected for exactly this reason — it would need a second manual stop action, which is the ticket's explicitly out-of-scope "stop control" (`M6-VUI-FE-133`) built for the wrong problem (stopping the *answer*, not the *capture*), or it would need to guess at a transcribing state ahead of any real signal, which is the failure mode the ticket names outright.

On `ScriptProcessorNode` versus `AudioWorklet`: `AudioWorklet` is the modern, non-deprecated API, and was the first choice. It was rejected for this ticket because it requires its own module file loaded via `audioContext.audioWorklet.addModule(url)` — a static asset the Next.js static export (`output: "export"`) would need to ship and version alongside the JS bundle, for a browser-only, capture-only, moderate-volume callback (one 4096-sample buffer at a time) where the main-thread jank `AudioWorklet` exists to avoid is not a real risk here. `ScriptProcessorNode` is deprecated but still implemented everywhere this product runs (Chromium, Firefox, Safari), needs no separate asset, and keeps the whole capture path in one file. If audio processing later needs to grow past simple downsampling (echo cancellation tuning, a real-time level meter needing sub-frame timing for `M6-VUI-FE-132`), that is the trigger to revisit — noted rather than guessed around now.

**Consequences:** A voice turn cannot be started or stopped by anything other than holding the mic control — no keyboard shortcut, no click-and-release-elsewhere-safely (pointer capture is not set, so a drag off the button while held also stops capture via `pointerleave`, deliberately, since an ambiguous pointer state defaulting to "still recording" would be the worse failure). `M6-VUI-FE-132`'s level meter reads the same `ScriptProcessorNode` callback this ticket already runs, not a new capture path. Reversing the `AudioWorklet` choice later means adding a public worklet asset and wiring `next.config.ts`/`web/public/` to ship it, not a small diff.

**Refs:** `docs/ux/voice.md` §3, §7; `docs/backlog/M6-i-can-speak-to-it.md` (`M6-VUI-FE-128a`, `-132`, `-133`); `web/lib/voice.ts`; `web/components/ask/voice-control.tsx`.

## 2026-09-20 — `M6-TTS-BE-131`: a synthesis failure degrades to text per-sentence, with no in-turn retry, rather than failing the turn or retrying against a model that will not come back mid-answer

**Decision:** `askwell.voice_tts._speak_answer` catches `SynthesisUnavailable`/`SynthesisFailed` at the point of each sentence's `/synthesize` call, instead of letting either propagate out of the poll loop. The first failure for a turn emits a `voice` WebSocket event carrying the reason and stops sending any further sentence to `/synthesize` for the rest of that turn — text generation continues untouched. No retry is attempted against `/synthesize` again within the same turn.

**Why:** Before this ticket, an uncaught `SynthesisUnavailable` unwound all the way out of `_speak_answer` into `askwell.voice_channel._run_driver`'s broad `except Exception`, which marked the *entire turn* `failed` — including the answer text that had already generated correctly, and leaving the turn's pending `messages` row stuck `running` (nothing updates it once `_speak_answer` raises rather than returning). That is a strictly worse outcome than the ticket's own stated one: "a voice failure never blocks an answer." Two alternatives to "stop trying for the rest of this turn" were considered and rejected. Retrying `/synthesize` before every subsequent sentence: rejected, because Kokoro either loaded at process startup or it did not — nothing about the few hundred milliseconds between one sentence and the next changes that, so a retry only adds latency to every remaining sentence of a doomed turn for no chance of success ("no automatic repair" is stated directly in the ticket's own Known Gaps). Recovering automatically mid-turn if a later sentence's call happened to succeed: also rejected, for the same reason in reverse — if Kokoro genuinely wasn't loaded, no request in the next few seconds changes that, so the added branch (and the event-ordering question of a "voice available again" event arriving after "voice unavailable" inside one turn, which `docs/ux/voice.md` §5 does not ask for) bought nothing. Recovery is scoped to the *next* turn instead, which needs no special-casing at all: each turn's first `/synthesize` call is a fresh, independent HTTP request regardless of what the turn before it did, so a later turn simply succeeds once the model is back — the "spoken without a reload" acceptance criterion falls out of the existing per-turn call shape rather than needing new code to detect the transition mid-flight.

**Consequences:** A `voice` event kind now exists on the WebSocket protocol (`api/src/askwell/voice_channel.py`), append-only in the same style `citation`/`fact_citation` were added — a frontend voice screen (not yet built) needs to render it as the "voice unavailable, note shown" state. `build_tts_driver` now owns one `availability: dict[str, bool]` shared across every turn it drives, which is also where the two log lines (`voice_synthesis_unavailable`, `voice_synthesis_recovered`) live — an operator greps these to see the transition, rather than needing a wall of per-sentence noise or a persisted counter. If this needed reversing (say, a future ticket wants an in-turn retry), the natural seam is `_speak_sentence` in `api/src/askwell/voice_tts.py` — it is the one place a sentence is attempted, caught, and reported.

**Refs:** `docs/ux/voice.md` §5, `docs/states-and-edge-cases.md` §5, `api/src/askwell/voice_tts.py`, `api/src/askwell/voice_channel.py`, `api/tests/test_voice_tts.py`.

## 2026-09-20 — `M6-TTS-BE-130`: natural source references built mechanically from the filename, not by a voice-only prompt variant; `askwell.ask._generate` reused directly rather than duplicated

**Decision:** Two calls. First — resolving issue #447 — the spoken answer names a cited document naturally ("from the supplier agreement 2024") by deriving the phrase from the citation's own `filename` in `api/src/askwell/voice_tts.py` (`_natural_reference`, `_spoken_form`), the first time a given document is cited in an answer, rather than by asking the model to phrase it in prose as part of generation. Second: sentence-streamed synthesis is wired by calling `askwell.ask`'s own `_generate` directly against a `_Turn` this module builds itself, polled the same way `askwell.ask._tail` polls for SSE, rather than re-implementing retrieval, SQL routing, citation and abstention logic a second time for voice.

**Why:** The issue's own recommendation (#447, option 1) was a voice-mode instruction added to `answer_composition.v1.md` asking the model to name sources in prose. Rejected: that prompt is shared by every mode — `compose()` takes no mode parameter, and giving it one just for voice would mean either a second prompt file synced by hand with the first (two texts drifting is exactly what a shared prompt in one file was chosen to prevent) or a conditional block inside the one file, still evaluated on every typed turn. Either way, the model would start naming sources in prose in the *typed* answer too, since the composed text is the same string the screen renders — directly breaking this ticket's own Validation Rule ("screen rendering is unchanged in voice mode") and `docs/ux/voice.md` §2's "voice changes the input and adds audio output; it removes nothing." A prompt change also triggers `AGENTS.md` §4's eval-run requirement, and there is no suite that scores *prose quality of a source mention* — building one to evaluate a one-ticket voice-only phrasing convenience was judged disproportionate to the ticket's own 3-4 hour estimate. The mechanical alternative is deterministic, testable without an eval run, adds nothing to the shared prompt, and — because it only fires the first time a document is cited in an answer, not on every marker — avoids the repetitiveness the issue itself worried option 2 would produce. The cost: the phrase is only as natural as a filename allows ("supplier agreement 2024", not "the 2024 supply agreement"), and only document citations get one at all — a memory-fact or schema-note citation has no filename to build a phrase from, and is simply spoken with the marker gone, same as every citation was before this ticket.

For generation: the alternative was a second implementation of retrieval, SQL routing, citation resolution, abstention and the tool loop, specialised for voice. Rejected outright — `ask.py` is roughly 2,400 lines precisely because none of that logic is simple, and a second copy would drift from the first on every future ticket that touches one but not the other. `ask.py`'s own comment on the `ON CONFLICT` insert already documents that "a caller driving `_generate` directly against a turn it built itself" is an established pattern — every stop/disconnection test in `test_ask_api.py` already does this — so this ticket is a second such caller, not a new one. The trade-off accepted: `voice_tts.py` imports three underscore-prefixed names from `ask.py` (`_generate`, `_Turn`) that carry no public-API stability guarantee; a future refactor of `ask.py`'s internals must keep this caller in mind even though nothing marks it as a caller from the outside.

**Consequences:** A future ticket that wants nicer spoken source phrasing (closer to the ticket's own "from the 2024 supply agreement" example) has to either give documents a display title distinct from their filename (no such field exists anywhere in the schema today) or accept the cost of a voice-only prompt variant and the eval run that comes with it — this decision does not close that door, it just declines to open it for a 3-4 hour ticket. If `ask.py`'s internal `_Turn`/`_generate` shape changes, `voice_tts.py` must be updated in the same change; nothing enforces that automatically beyond `mypy --strict` catching a signature mismatch.

**Refs:** `docs/ux/voice.md` §6; `AGENTS.md` §3 C4, §4; `api/src/askwell/voice_tts.py`; `api/src/askwell/agent/claims.py` (`segment_sentences`, `Sentence`); `api/src/askwell/voice/synthesize.py`; `api/src/askwell/voice/service.py` (`/synthesize`); `api/src/askwell/voice_stt.py` (`on_transcript`); `api/src/askwell/voice_channel.py` (`citation`/`fact_citation` events, output wire format); issue #447.

## 2026-09-20 — `M6-STT-BE-128`: VAD scored stateless per frame over HTTP; the pause threshold is a reasoned guess, not a measured one

**Decision:** Two calls. First: `askwell.voice.vad.score_frames` (inside the `voice` container) resets Silero's recurrent state (`h`, `c`) to zero on every 512-sample frame it scores, rather than carrying it forward between frames the way Silero's own streaming example does. Second: `Settings.voice_vad_pause_ms` defaults to 700ms, chosen by reasoning about typical speech pauses rather than by measuring any.

**Why:** For statelessness — the alternative was keeping a per-turn VAD session (the recurrent state) alive inside the `voice` container, keyed by `turn_id`, the same shape `askwell.voice_channel._turns` already uses for turns themselves. Rejected because it would be the first place either container keeps state tied to a turn's *lifetime* rather than to one request: `/transcribe` is already a single stateless call per turn, and a stateful `/vad` would need its own cleanup path (what retires a session if a turn ends via `stop` rather than exhausting it, or if a connection drops mid-turn — the reconnect story `voice_channel`'s own module docstring already treats as "a delivery problem, not a generation one") for a component with no existing lifecycle hook to hang that off. Zero state per frame costs per-frame precision, but `askwell.voice_turn_detection` never acts on one frame — it accumulates several consecutive low-probability frames (`voice_vad_pause_ms` worth) before treating that as a pause, so a single noisy frame reclassified near a state-reset boundary does not flip the outcome. The HTTP round trip per audio frame this implies (one `/vad` call roughly every 32ms of audio, batched slightly by the caller's own frame-alignment buffering) was accepted rather than batching many frames per call, because batching would only delay when a pause is detected by exactly the batch size — working against the latency the whole voice feature is graded on (`docs/ux/voice.md` §1's 3.5s/8s budget).

For the threshold: 700ms sits above a typical in-sentence breath (150-300ms, by ear, not measured) and below the pause most people leave while composing a follow-on clause. The alternative — leaving it unset until real audio exists to tune against — was rejected because the acceptance criteria for this ticket explicitly asks for "a pause after speech closes the turn" as a working behaviour today, and an unset threshold is not a working behaviour. The honest position, stated in both `config.py`'s own comment and here, is that no recorded-speech fixtures exist in this environment (no microphone, no bundled audio, and `M6-VUI-FE-132`'s composer control — the first real capture code — has not landed) to tune this against, so 700ms is a documented starting point the ticket's own testing notes already anticipate revisiting: "if it is unusable [in a noisy room], that is the open question being answered."

**Consequences:** `M6-VUI-FE-132` (the composer control) inherits a working pause-close behind whatever real microphone capture it adds, and should report back — as a follow-up issue, not silently — whether 700ms holds up against real speech, particularly the "long thinking pause mid-question" edge case this ticket's own acceptance criteria calls out. If a future requirement needs frame-level precision after all (e.g., the threshold turns out too coarse in a noisy room), reversing the stateless choice means adding the per-turn session lifecycle described above to `voice`, not just changing a number.

**Refs:** `AGENTS.md` §3 C1 (no model download at runtime; `voice`'s own no-egress posture); `docs/ux/voice.md` §3, §5, §7; `docs/backlog/M6-i-can-speak-to-it.md` (`M6-STT-BE-128`, `M6-VUI-FE-132`); `api/src/askwell/voice/vad.py`; `api/src/askwell/voice_turn_detection.py`; `api/src/askwell/voice_channel.py`; `api/src/askwell/config.py`; issue #448.

## 2026-09-20 — `M6-STT-BE-127`: 16 kHz mono PCM16 over the wire; transcription persists this ticket, not the next one

**Decision:** Two calls, both made with no frontend voice code yet to consult. First: the voice WebSocket's audio frames are **16 kHz mono, 16-bit signed PCM, little-endian** — decided in `api/src/askwell/voice/transcribe.py`'s own docstring, since nothing upstream (`M6-AUDIO-API-126`'s transport carries opaque bytes) had fixed a format yet and Whisper's `WhisperModel.transcribe()` requires exactly this when given a numpy array rather than a file path. Second: this ticket writes the transcript to `messages` and `audit_interactions` itself (`api/src/askwell/voice_stt.py`'s `_store_turn`, a new `conversations` row stamped `mode='voice'` when none is given) rather than leaving persistence for `M6-TTS-BE-130`, which actually wires a voice turn into `askwell.ask`'s generation. A turn with no speech, or a turn that closed with no audio captured at all, stores nothing — `M6-STT-BE-128`'s own stated rule ("no interaction record because nothing was asked") applied here even though that ticket has not landed.

**Why:** For the wire format, the alternative was leaving it opaque until `M6-VUI-FE-132` (the composer control) forces a real answer — rejected because `M6-STT-BE-128`'s Silero VAD needs the identical format regardless, and choosing per-ticket would mean either a resample step at every future boundary or two competing "first" decisions racing to define the contract. 16 kHz mono PCM16 is Silero's own native input and what `faster-whisper` accepts as a raw array with zero decoding, so one format serves capture, VAD and Whisper with nothing in between. The alternative to raw PCM was accepting compressed audio (webm/opus, what a browser's `MediaRecorder` produces with no extra code) and decoding it in the `voice` container — rejected because it would add ffmpeg or a codec library to a container whose entire justification (`M6-AUDIO-DEPLOY-125`'s decision entry) was reusing the existing image for three ML dependencies and nothing else; the cost is pushed onto whatever frontend capture code `M6-VUI-FE-132` writes, which needs a `MediaStreamTrackProcessor`/`AudioWorklet` step to produce PCM16 instead of just handing `MediaRecorder`'s blob to the socket — more frontend work, avoided container dependency.

For persistence, the alternative was treating this ticket as STT-only and leaving `conversations`/`messages`/audit entirely to `M6-TTS-BE-130`, mirroring how `M6-AUDIO-API-126` deliberately left every table untouched. Rejected because this ticket's own Acceptance Criteria is explicit — "the transcript is stored with the turn," and "the transcript is part of the interaction record" — and because C6 makes an unrecorded interaction a bug, not a limitation: a spoken question that got transcribed and then vanished if `M6-TTS-BE-130` never wired it up (a real possibility — TTS/answer-generation could be reprioritised, or generation could fail before ever reading the transcript) would be a genuine question the user asked, with no record anywhere it happened. `M6-AUDIO-API-126` could defer persistence because it had no transcript to persist; this ticket does.

**Consequences:** `M6-STT-BE-128` (turn detection) and `M6-VUI-FE-132` (the composer control, first real capture code) must both target 16 kHz mono PCM16 — changing the format now means touching `voice/transcribe.py`, this ticket's tests, and whatever capture code exists by then, together. `M6-TTS-BE-130` inherits a `messages` row per voice turn (role `user`, possibly empty content for the unsupported-language case) and an existing `conversations` row it should reuse via `VoiceTurn.conversation_id` rather than creating a second one — it must not re-derive persistence logic this ticket already owns. `voice_channel.py`'s `_Event` moved from a fixed `text` field to a generic `fields: dict[str, Any]`, so a future event kind (e.g. `M6-TTS-BE-130`'s own audio-ready signal, if it needs one beyond the existing `status` message) has a shape to extend rather than a second ad hoc field to add.

**Refs:** `AGENTS.md` §3 C1 (no ffmpeg/codec dependency reasoning ties back to the container's no-egress, minimal-surface design), C6; `docs/backlog/M6-i-can-speak-to-it.md` (`M6-STT-BE-127`, `M6-STT-BE-128`, `M6-TTS-BE-130`); `docs/ux/voice.md` §5, §7; `api/src/askwell/voice/transcribe.py`; `api/src/askwell/voice_stt.py`; `api/src/askwell/voice_channel.py`; issue #445.

---

## 2026-09-20 — `M6-AUDIO-API-126`: the voice channel is mounted on `api`, not `voice`; audio has no history, only a queue

**Decision:** The bidirectional voice WebSocket (`/voice/ws`) is a new module on the `api` service (`api/src/askwell/voice_channel.py`), registered in `api/src/askwell/app.py` alongside `register_ask` and the rest — not an endpoint on the `voice` container that `M6-AUDIO-DEPLOY-125` built. A `VoiceTurn` lives independently of any one connection, keyed by a server-minted `turn_id`, mirroring `askwell.ask`'s `_Turn`/`_turns`. `transcript` and `text` are kept as running strings, resent in full on every (re)attach; `audio_out` is a bounded `asyncio.Queue` with **no replay history at all** — a reconnect just resumes draining the same queue instance from wherever it currently is. There is no wiring into `conversations`/`messages` or the audit log in this ticket; `register_voice_channel` takes an injectable `driver`, and production runs a placeholder that drains incoming audio and closes the turn on the client's `end` signal without producing any transcript, text or audio.

**Why:** Mounting on `voice` instead of `api` was the alternative — it is where the STT/TTS models will eventually live, so it reads as the more "natural" home for a speech channel. It was rejected because `docs/architecture.md` §1/§2 already locks down that **only `api` is reachable from outside the machine** — `voice` sits on the `internal` network with no route to the host at all (`compose.yaml`, and confirmed live: `scripts/verify-localhost-binding.sh` shows `voice` publishing nothing). Exposing `voice` to the host to serve this channel would reopen a decision the architecture doc already closed, for a ticket whose own scope is transport, not model-serving; the eventual STT/TTS tickets can have `api` call into `voice` over the internal network exactly the way a real inference call would, without `voice` ever facing the browser.

Keeping audio out of any replayable event history — rather than mirroring `askwell.ask`'s own "replay every event from the start on reconnect" pattern — was the harder call. `askwell.ask._tail` replays a whole SSE turn from index 0 on every reconnect because text is cheap and doing so is simpler than tracking what a given browser already saw. Audio is not cheap in the same way, and the ticket's own edge case is explicit that a reconnect must not replay from the start. The alternative considered was a bounded ring buffer of recent chunks a reconnecting connection could partially replay from — rejected as unnecessary complexity: a plain queue, with the driver as sole producer and at most one connection draining it at a time, already gives the right behaviour for free (whatever was already dequeued is gone; whatever was not is exactly the "remaining audio" the reconnect should get) with no bookkeeping of per-connection delivery state. `transcript`/`text`, by contrast, are kept in full because they are small and "the text is complete regardless" is an explicit requirement, not an optimisation.

Not wiring `conversations`/`messages`/audit persistence in this ticket was a scope decision, not an oversight: there is no real transcript or answer to store yet (`M6-STT-BE-127` and `M6-TTS-BE-130` haven't landed), and `M6-STT-BE-127`'s own Acceptance Criteria already states "the transcript is stored with the turn" and lists it under Audit/Logging Requirements — building that persistence now would mean building it against content this ticket has no way to produce, and building it twice once real content exists.

**Consequences:** `M6-STT-BE-127`/`M6-TTS-BE-130` must supply a real `driver` (the injection point already exists) and, per their own acceptance criteria, the `conversations`/`messages`/audit wiring this ticket deliberately left out. If a future requirement needs partial audio replay after all (e.g. a UI that wants to re-render the last few seconds on reconnect), the queue-only design has nothing to build that from — reversing this would mean adding a bounded ring buffer alongside the queue, not changing the queue itself.

**Refs:** `AGENTS.md` §3 C1; `docs/architecture.md` §1, §2; `docs/ux/voice.md` §5, §7; `docs/backlog/M6-i-can-speak-to-it.md` (`M6-AUDIO-API-126`, `M6-STT-BE-127`); `api/src/askwell/voice_channel.py`; `api/src/askwell/ask.py` (`_Turn`, `_tail`).

---

## 2026-09-20 — `M6-AUDIO-DEPLOY-125`: voice is a container with no egress, and VAD has no health line of its own

**Decision:** The voice container reuses the existing `askwell` package and image rather than getting its own Dockerfile/pyproject/lockfile — same shape as `egress-proxy` and `inference-bridge`, a new `askwell-voice` entrypoint (`api/src/askwell/voice/service.py`) added to the one `api/Dockerfile` already built. It sits on the `internal` network **only** — not `egress`, unlike `api` and `worker` — because nothing in its scope, now or in `M6-AUDIO-API-126`, ever has a legitimate reason to leave the machine. Silero VAD is loaded through `onnxruntime` directly rather than the `silero-vad` PyPI package (which pulls in `torch`/`torchaudio` for a model this small), and its health is folded into the `transcription` line rather than reported as a third top-level state: nothing in the product asks "is voice activity detection available" as its own question, and Whisper cannot usefully transcribe without VAD gating the stream ahead of it, so a reader debugging "transcription unavailable" needs to see whichever of the two is actually the cause, not a third field to cross-reference.

**Why:** A second Dockerfile and lockfile for three ML dependencies (`faster-whisper`, `onnxruntime`, `kokoro-onnx`) was the alternative seriously considered — the standard reason to isolate a service is a dependency conflict or a wildly different base image, and neither applies: `uv lock` resolved all three alongside the existing dependency set with no conflicts (verified — `scripts/dev.sh lock` ran clean, `scripts/dev.sh build-api` built the image, `scripts/dev.sh check` passed 852/1 against it), and none needs a different Python or a different OS package set from `api`. Paying the cost of two build pipelines for isolation nothing here needs would be exactly the kind of scaffolding-ahead `AGENTS.md` §4 warns against. Denying `voice` the `egress` network is stricter than `api`/`worker` (which reach the proxy for online-AI and web search) and was a deliberate widening of C1 for this one service rather than an oversight — `docs/decisions.md`'s own "no escalation to the web from voice" entry already forecloses every legitimate reason voice would need a route out, so giving it one anyway would be attack surface with no corresponding feature ever using it. Folding VAD into `transcription` rather than exposing three top-level states was chosen over a literal one-model-one-line mapping because the ticket's own Acceptance Criteria names exactly two surfaces — "transcription and synthesis health separately" — and a third field with no consumer would be exactly the kind of structure invented to satisfy a word rather than a requirement, the same failure mode called out in the 2026-09-x memory-grouping entry elsewhere in this log.

A related gap surfaced rather than worked around: `Settings.database_url`, `sandbox_database_url`, `sandbox_owner_password` and `sandbox_readonly_password` are all required with no default, yet `compose.yaml`'s existing `egress-proxy` service does not set the three sandbox variables — meaning that service's `load_settings()` call should fail validation as configured today. `voice` was given the full set defensively so it actually boots (verified live: `podman compose up -d voice` started, logged `voice_startup`, and answered `GET /health`), but `egress-proxy`'s own gap was not touched — investigating and fixing a pre-existing service outside this ticket's scope would have been exactly the kind of undiscussed batching `AGENTS.md` §4 warns against for a single ticket. Filed as a follow-up rather than silently carried forward.

**Consequences:** Adding a fourth ML-heavy dependency to `voice`'s scope later (e.g. a bigger Whisper tier for a `workstation` profile) grows the shared `api` image for every service that uses it, not just `voice` — reversing this into a dedicated image is possible later but would need its own Dockerfile, lockfile and CI job at that point, not before. If `egress-proxy`'s missing sandbox variables turn out to be a live bug rather than a `Settings()` default I have not found, it is untouched by this change and still needs its own fix.

**Refs:** `AGENTS.md` §3 C1, §4; `docs/architecture.md` §2.1; `docs/decisions.md`'s "no escalation to the web from voice" entry (2026-08-xx, Phase 5 voice section); `compose.yaml`; `api/src/askwell/voice/`.
