## 2026-09-20 — `M5-TRACE-FE-122`: the retrieval threshold becomes a runtime setting, not just a deploy-time one

**Decision:** `Settings.retrieval_score_threshold` stays the shipped, deploy-time default (`0.65`, guarded by `eval/tests/test_abstain.py`'s own pin), but it is no longer the only value in force. A new `askwell.retrieve.get_retrieval_threshold`/`set_retrieval_threshold` pair reads and writes an override in the generic `settings` key/value table — the same shape `askwell.clarify.get_clarification_cap`/`set_clarification_cap` already established for a per-install preference — and `retrieve()` now calls `get_retrieval_threshold()` instead of reading `settings.retrieval_score_threshold` directly. `set_retrieval_threshold` is the only way the value changes, and it always writes an `audit_decisions` record with the old and new value as strings (floats are not hashable payloads, `askwell.audit.canonical_payload`'s own rule). One HTTP endpoint pair, `GET`/`POST /settings/retrieval-threshold`, is reached from both the abstention trace's near-miss control and the settings screen.

**Why:** `M5-TRACE-FE-122`'s own Acceptance Criteria requires a real control the user can act on — "lower the threshold and re-ask the question — confirm it now answers" — which a deploy-time `.env` value cannot satisfy without a restart, and the ticket is explicit that this is a single-user product with no operator to restart anything on the user's behalf. The alternative considered was leaving the threshold as pure `Settings` and having this ticket's control just *display* the consequence without actually being able to change anything — rejected outright, since a control that cannot act is not a control, and the ticket's own cold-start walkthrough names re-asking and getting a different answer as the thing to verify. Reusing the clarification-cap shape (settings-table override, decisions record on every change) rather than inventing a new mechanism was the ticket's own "prefer what exists" instruction (`AGENTS.md` §4) — the two are the same kind of thing, a per-install preference that must never change silently. Writing the decisions payload as strings rather than floats was found while testing, not designed up front: `canonical_payload` already refuses a float for exactly the reason `M2-ABSTAIN-RET-053`'s own comment states elsewhere in this codebase — a `jsonb` round trip does not preserve a float identically, and a verifier that then reports tampering that never happened is worse than one that never ran.

**Consequences:** The abstention decision in `askwell.ask` is unaffected in shape — it still just reads `result.threshold` — but that value can now legitimately differ turn to turn if someone changes the setting between two questions, which is exactly the point and exactly why `docs/ux/trace.md`'s own Assumption ("past turns keep their recorded threshold") matters: a stored trace's `threshold` field is never recomputed, so an old abstention still explains itself with the number that produced it. Reversing this — going back to a `Settings`-only threshold — would mean removing the two HTTP routes and the settings-table read in `retrieve()`, and would break the ticket's own Acceptance Criteria that a change take effect on the very next question.

**Refs:** `docs/backlog/M5-it-handles-harder-questions.md`'s `M5-TRACE-FE-122`; `docs/ux/trace.md` §4; `docs/ux/settings.md` §2; `api/src/askwell/retrieve.py`; `api/tests/test_retrieve_records.py`; `eval/tests/test_abstain.py::test_retrieval_score_threshold_default_has_not_been_quietly_lowered` (the existing guard, unchanged, over the deploy-time default this decision leaves alone).

---

## 2026-09-20 — `M5-LOOP-BE-117a`: live tool-call step events wire only the `"end"` phase into the Ask screen for now

**Decision:** `run_tool_loop`'s new `on_tool_call` observer fires both `"start"` and `"end"` per call, and both are tested in `api/tests/test_loop.py`. But `askwell.ask`'s two call sites — the actual wiring into `turn.emit("step", ...)`, the SSE channel the Ask screen renders — only forward the `"end"` phase. The pre-loop generic label ("Working through this in steps." / "Continuing where it left off.") stays exactly as it was, unremoved.

**Why:** Issue #420, filed against this exact ticket before it merged, named the trap directly: the shipped frontend (`web/lib/ask.ts`'s `applyAskEvent`, `web/components/ask/ask-screen.tsx`'s `turn.steps.map(s => s.label).join(" · ")`) has no phase or call-id discriminator yet — that discriminator is explicitly `M5-LOOP-FE-118`'s scope, not this ticket's — so it appends every `step` event's label unconditionally. Wiring both phases today would turn one tool call into two near-identical fragments ("Called document_search. · Called document_search.") live in the Ask screen, on every hybrid or continuation turn — a real, visible regression from the single deduplicated label per tool this replaced, and worse than the "working for twenty seconds looks hung" problem the ticket exists to fix. The issue's own recommended option (2 of 3) was to surface only one phase until the frontend can tell them apart; option 1 (land `FE-118` in the same change) was rejected because this ticket's scope is explicitly backend-only and bundling would blur what each ticket actually shipped; option 3 (ship both phases and accept the regression) was rejected outright as strictly worse than what it replaced. `"end"` was chosen over `"start"` because "Called X." is only true once the call has actually returned — the ticket's own stated business value ("the label can be true rather than reconstructed") is about honesty at completion, not about announcing an attempt. The pre-loop generic label was kept, not dropped, because removing it would leave nothing on screen between the question landing and the first call finishing — reopening exactly the "indistinguishable from hung" gap this ticket's business-value section names, for a turn's first call specifically.

**Consequences:** The Ask screen shows a slightly later signal than a full start+end wiring would (a call's presence is only known once it finishes, not when it begins) until `M5-LOOP-FE-118` ships a discriminator `applyAskEvent` can dedupe on. At that point `_emit_tool_call_step` in `api/src/askwell/ask.py` should switch to forwarding both phases — the observer and its tests already support that with no further backend change. Reversing this without `FE-118` landed first reintroduces the doubled-label regression.

**Refs:** Issue #420. `api/src/askwell/agent/loop.py` (`ToolCallEvent`, `ToolCallObserver`, `_notify`, `on_tool_call`). `api/src/askwell/ask.py` (`_emit_tool_call_step`). `api/tests/test_loop.py`.

## 2026-09-20 — `M5-EVAL-TEST-124`: tool-choice grading is exact-set match, parallel dispatch is proven from `LoopStep.iteration`, not asserted from a passing answer

**Decision:** `eval.tool_selection.tool_choice_score` scores a task correct only if the distinct tools the loop actually called equal one of its accepted routes exactly — not a subset (missing a needed tool) and not a superset (an extra, unneeded one). `parallel_achieved` proves parallel dispatch happened by grouping non-deduplicated `LoopStep`s by `iteration` and checking that at least one iteration has two or more — the one trace shape `askwell.agent.loop`'s `asyncio.gather` batch produces — rather than inferring it from the final answer being correct.

**Why:** Subset matching (accepting the model calling extra tools beyond the accepted route, so long as the needed ones were among them) was the obvious alternative and was rejected because it cannot score the ticket's own "tempted by an unnecessary database call" edge case at all — a task built specifically to check that a reflexive extra tool call does not happen would always pass under subset matching, since the required tool is still in there. Exact-set match makes the extra call a failure the same way a missing one is. For parallel dispatch, the alternative was scoring it from the final answer alone (if the answer correctly used both pieces of information, assume both tools ran together) — rejected because a correct answer says nothing about *when* the two calls happened; a model that called them one iteration apart, sequentially, can produce an identical answer to one that batched them, and the whole point of this suite's parallel category is telling those two apart. `LoopStep.iteration`, already recorded by `askwell.agent.loop` for its own trace, is the one field that actually distinguishes them, so grading reads it directly rather than adding new instrumentation to the loop for this suite's sake.

**Consequences:** A task in `expected_tool_routes` with more than one entry (the "two acceptable tool routes" edge case) requires listing every accepted route explicitly — there is no "at least these tools" mode, so a new acceptable route found later is a suite-file edit, not a code change. `require_parallel` zeroes a task's tool-choice component outright if `parallel_achieved` is false, even when the right tools were eventually both called and the final answer is correct — by design, per the ticket's own "removing parallel dispatch fails the parallel tasks specifically" acceptance criterion, verified in `eval/tests/test_tool_selection.py` without needing a live model.

**Refs:** `M5-EVAL-TEST-124`, `M5-LOOP-BE-115`, `M5-LOOP-BE-116`, `docs/build-plan.md`'s quality gate, `eval/tool_selection.py`, `eval/suites/tool_selection.v1.json`, `eval/tests/test_tool_selection.py`.

## 2026-09-20 — `M5-LOOP-BE-117`: `messages.trace` is trimmed by id, in the same transaction as the write that triggers the rotation — not by a separate sweep

**Decision:** `TraceRing.prune()` now returns the message ids of the trace files it actually dropped, `TraceRing.write()` surfaces them on a new `TraceWriteResult`, and `askwell.ask._trim_rotated_traces` clears `trace.steps` (setting `trace_rotated: true`) for exactly those rows, called inline inside the same `session_scope` block that persists the triggering turn. Every other trace field is left alone. A new `TRACE_STEP_BOUND` (50) caps how many steps one turn's own trace can carry, truncating with `steps_truncated: true` stated inside the trace rather than silently cutting it.

**Why:** `docs/architecture.md` §7.1 has said since it was written that "`messages.trace` is trimmed with them [the file ring buffer]," but nothing ever did it — `TraceRing` only ever managed files on disk, and the DB column kept every turn's full step detail forever, growing without the bound the architecture document already promised. Two designs were considered for closing this. A periodic sweep (a worker job comparing `messages.trace` against what still exists in the ring buffer directory) was rejected: it needs its own schedule, its own failure handling, and a window during which the DB and the file store disagree about what "still has a trace" means — exactly the kind of drift the architecture note is trying to rule out. Trimming inline, driven by `prune()`'s own return value, has no such window: the ids trimmed are exactly the ids that just left disk, in the same write that caused it. The cost is that `TraceRing.write()`'s return type changed from `Path | None` to `TraceWriteResult` — a breaking change to a small, this-repository-only API, accepted because every caller either ignored the return value already or needed updating anyway. A separate `messages.trace` size cap (`TRACE_STEP_BOUND`) was added alongside this rather than folded into the ring buffer's own byte cap, because the two protect against different things: the ring buffer's cap is disk pressure across many turns, while `TRACE_STEP_BOUND` is one pathological turn (a tool loop that somehow accumulates far more steps than `CALL_CEILING` should allow) — the ticket's own named edge case, and worth catching even before that turn's trace ever reaches the ring buffer at all.

**Consequences:** A reopened old turn whose trace has rotated out renders its `status`/`backend`/citations exactly as before — only the step-by-step detail (`trace.md`'s numbered sequence) is gone, replaced by the "Trace unavailable" state (`docs/ux/trace.md` §5) once `M5-TRACE-FE-119` builds it. `_sql_query_from_trace` (`M4-RESULT-FE-110`'s disclosure reconstruction) returns `None` for a rotated turn's rejected/failed query, since that walk reads `trace.steps` — accepted, since the same trace-md §5 rule already treats debugging detail as rotating while the answer itself does not, and a rejected-query disclosure is exactly the kind of debugging detail the architecture note describes. Raising `TRACE_STEP_BOUND` later is a one-line change, not a migration, since nothing downstream assumes a specific count.

**Refs:** `M5-LOOP-BE-117`, `docs/architecture.md` §7.1, `docs/ux/trace.md` §5, `api/src/askwell/traces.py` (`TraceWriteResult`, `TraceRing.prune`), `api/src/askwell/ask.py` (`_trim_rotated_traces`, `TRACE_STEP_BOUND`, `_bound_trace_steps`), `api/src/askwell/agent/sql_generate.py` (`GeneratedQuery.schema_lookup_ms`), `api/tests/test_traces.py`, `api/tests/test_ask_api.py`, `api/tests/test_ask_sql.py`.

## 2026-09-20 — `M5-LOOP-BE-116`: a ceiling stop counts calls, not iterations; `Continue` gets a fresh budget and no cross-turn dedup

**Decision:** `askwell.agent.loop.CALL_CEILING` (8) counts actual fresh tool executions across a turn, not model iterations — a batch that would cross it is truncated at it, and the cut calls are recorded as `pending_calls` rather than dropped. `Continue` (`LoopResult.continuation` → `messages.trace.loop_continuation` → `run_tool_loop(resume=...)`) starts the new turn's own `CALL_CEILING` at zero rather than inheriting the stopped turn's count, and does not attempt cross-turn tool-call deduplication — it only seeds the `<tool-result>` history back into the prompt.

**Why:** Counting iterations instead of calls was the obvious alternative and was rejected because it does not match what the ticket actually protects against: a model that asks for four independent lookups in one array should not be capped as though it made four separate round trips, and a turn that quietly asks for one call at a time, eight times over, is exactly as expensive as one that batches them — the ceiling has to see the same cost either way or a model that happens to batch gets an unfair 8x the real budget. Counting calls also produces the two distinct trace shapes `docs/ux/trace.md` §5 and this ticket's own edge cases ask for without extra bookkeeping: a mid-batch truncation gives an exact `pending_calls` list for free (the calls that literally did not run), while an exact-fit stop (no batch ever crossed the line) has no such list, so `_stop_at_ceiling` spends one extra model call asking what it was about to do — never more than one, since that answer, whatever it is, ends the turn. On `Continue`: giving the new turn a shared, carried-over counter was considered and rejected, because "start a new turn" would then mean nothing — a corpus needing 12 calls total would still fail after one `Continue`, defeating the point of offering it at all; a fresh budget per turn is what "new turn" has to mean for the feature to do anything. Reconstructing dedup across turns (persisting each tool's raw content a second time, keyed by call, so a repeat in the new turn skips re-execution) was considered and rejected as not worth its own weight: seeding `history_blocks` back into the prompt already puts every gathered result in front of the model, and the loop's own prompt already tells it to use what it has rather than re-ask — the case this would prevent (the model ignoring its own context and re-asking anyway) is rare enough that paying to build a second persistence path for it isn't justified yet.

**Consequences:** A turn that hits the ceiling via a single oversized batch and one that hits it one call at a time report the same `stopped_reason` but arrive at their `pending_calls` differently — a mid-batch truncation never spends the extra forced model call `_stop_at_ceiling` otherwise makes, since the truncated calls are already known. A resumed (`Continue`) turn that itself asks for a tool the first turn already ran will re-run it rather than reusing the cached result — accepted cost, not a bug, per the *Why* above; if telemetry later shows this happening often enough to matter, the fix is persisting `_CallRecord.result_content_json` alongside `history_blocks` in `LoopContinuation`, not changing the ceiling's own counting. A second ceiling stop in the same `Continue` chain (`continuation_count >= 1`) does not offer a third continuation at all — the chain is capped at one `Continue` by construction, not by a configurable limit.

**Refs:** `M5-LOOP-BE-116`, `M5-LOOP-BE-115`, `docs/architecture.md` §10, `docs/ux/ask.md` §5, `docs/ux/trace.md` §5, `api/src/askwell/agent/loop.py` (`CALL_CEILING`, `_stop_at_ceiling`, `LoopContinuation`), `api/src/askwell/ask.py` (`AskRequest.continue_from`, `_load_loop_continuation`), `api/tests/test_loop.py`.

## 2026-09-20 — `M5-LOOP-BE-115`: the tool loop is only offered a genuinely hybrid corpus, not every turn

**Decision:** `askwell.ask._run_generation` tries `askwell.agent.loop.run_tool_loop` only when `_has_hybrid_sources` is true — at least one `ready` document and one `ready` database-backed source both exist, and only for a conversation-wide question (`source_id is None`). Every other turn — the ordinary case, one kind of source or none — falls through to `_run_sql_turn` and the single-shot document path completely unchanged from before this ticket.

**Why:** The ticket's own reading of "wire the loop into `_run_generation` ahead of the single-shot document path" is unconditional — try the loop on every turn that `_run_sql_turn` didn't already answer, and fall back to it only on a malformed model response. That was audited by a set of open questions filed against this ticket before it closed (#407, #408, #409). #409 is the one that matters here: the loop's own answer branch does not run memory retrieval, `split_partial_answer`, or `split_conflict_answer` — those are real, tested, shipped behaviours (`M2-PARTIAL-BE-057`, `-059`) that only the single-shot document path below still calls. An unconditional loop would have intercepted *every* ordinary document question — the common case, not a rare hybrid one — and silently answered it without partial-coverage or conflict detection, with no test catching it because `test_ask_api.py` never gave the model a chance to call `document_search` and stop there instead of running the existing path. Building real parity for the loop (issue #409's option 2 — running partial/conflict detection against the loop's own answer text, threading memory into its prompt) is a second body of work the same size as this ticket, gated behind loop citations (#407) landing first, since both need the same claim-segmentation machinery. Narrowing *when* the loop is offered at all — #409's own "near-term fix" recommendation — was the cheaper, correct-now alternative: a corpus that is only documents or only a database already has a fully-featured path, so scoping the loop to the one case neither of those alone can answer (the ticket's own worked example) costs nothing on ordinary traffic and loses nothing the loop would have added there anyway, since a single-kind corpus never needed two tools in the first place.

**Consequences:** A hybrid question is answered without citations resolving into the `citations` table (#407, its own ticket) or memory/partial/conflict participating (#409's remaining gap) until those land — an accepted, tracked narrowing, not a silent one. `_run_sql_turn` is still tried first on every turn regardless of corpus shape, so a hybrid question whose database half alone matches a schema note strongly enough can still short-circuit before the loop is ever reached (#408, left open — its own recommendation is to revisit once #407 lands, not before). Widening `_has_hybrid_sources` or removing it later must be checked against `test_ask_api.py`'s and `test_ask_sql.py`'s existing fixtures first — neither currently creates a database-backed source alongside a document one, which is the only reason this gate is safe to add without touching either file; that stops being true the moment a test does both.

**Refs:** `M5-LOOP-BE-115`, `M5-TOOLS-BE-113`, `M5-TOOLS-BE-114`, issues #407, #408, #409, `api/src/askwell/agent/loop.py`, `api/src/askwell/ask.py` (`_has_hybrid_sources`, the loop branch in `_run_generation`), `api/tests/test_loop.py`, `api/tests/test_ask_hybrid_sources_db.py`.

## 2026-09-19 — `M5-TOOLS-BE-114`: tool-result flagging lives in `call_tool` itself, not in the loop that does not exist yet

**Decision:** `askwell.agent.tools._step` computes `injection_flagged`/`injection_patterns` on every `ToolStep`, unconditionally, by flattening a tool result's content to its string leaves and running it through `askwell.agent.compose.flag_injection_text` — the same heuristic a retrieved passage already goes through, not a second copy of it. Delimitation gets the same treatment: `askwell.agent.compose.delimit_tool_result` wraps a result in an unforgeable `<tool-result>` block, and the prompt file's C7 standing statement now names `<tool-result>` explicitly alongside `<retrieved-content>`.

**Why:** `M5-TOOLS-BE-113` deliberately left the tool registry unwired — nothing calls `call_tool` more than once per turn until `M5-LOOP-BE-115` exists. The tempting shortcut here was to defer flagging to that same future ticket, on the reasoning that a flag nobody reads yet is pointless work. Rejected: C7 is a constraint on what the model sees, not on what the loop does with it, and a flag computed lazily by whichever caller shows up first is exactly the kind of thing that gets forgotten once that caller is under its own deadline pressure. Computing it in `call_tool` — the one place every tool result already passes through on its way to a trace step — means the loop inherits a correct, tested flag for free instead of having to remember to add one. Escaping was the other real decision: `delimit_candidates` (`M1-ASK-BE-037`) does not escape a candidate's own content against the `<retrieved-content>` tag appearing inside it, because document text forging an XML-looking tag is a much narrower threat than a database row an attacker fully controls (C3's untrusted-dump case). Tool results include exactly that case, so `delimit_tool_result` escapes unconditionally rather than matching the candidate path's lighter-touch behaviour — the two delimiters are allowed to diverge here, and this entry is that divergence written down before it looks like an inconsistency.

**Consequences:** A future prompt actually assembled with tool-result blocks (the loop's own job) gets delimitation and flagging for free, already tested against escaping and nested-content edge cases. If `M5-LOOP-BE-115` needs multiple tool results indexed into one prompt, `delimit_tool_result`'s `index` argument is already threaded through per-call, the same shape `delimit_candidates` uses. Reopening this would mean either moving the flag computation into the loop (rejected above) or duplicating the escape behaviour onto `delimit_candidates` retroactively — neither is free, so a reason to reopen it should be a real incident, not a preference.

**Refs:** `M5-TOOLS-BE-114`, `M5-TOOLS-BE-113`, `M1-ASK-BE-037`, `api/src/askwell/agent/tools.py`, `api/src/askwell/agent/compose.py`, `api/src/askwell/agent/prompts/answer_composition.v1.md`, `api/tests/test_tools.py`, `api/tests/test_compose.py`, `docs/architecture.md` §9, `docs/ux/trace.md` §3, `docs/states-and-edge-cases.md` §2.

---

## 2026-09-19 — `M5-TOOLS-BE-113`: the tool registry re-orchestrates the checked SQL chain rather than sharing `askwell.ask._run_sql_turn`

**Decision:** `askwell.agent.tools._database_query` calls the same underlying stages `askwell.ask._run_sql_turn` already calls — `generate_candidate_query` → `validate_query` → `inject_limit` → `dry_run_*` → `execute_checked_*` — with its own orchestration, rather than extracting `_run_sql_turn` into a function both modules share.

**Why:** The alternative — pulling `_run_sql_turn`'s body out of `askwell/ask.py` into a shared module both call — was considered and rejected for this ticket specifically. `ask.py` is a large, already-tested, currently-live turn path (`POST /ask`'s single-shot flow, `M4-SQL-BE-108a`); refactoring it to serve a caller that does not exist yet (the loop, `M5-LOOP-BE-115`) trades a small amount of duplicated wiring for real regression risk on the one path real turns already go through, for no present benefit — nothing calls the registry yet. What is *not* duplicated is every stage that actually enforces something: C2 validation lives in exactly one place (`askwell.sql.validate`), as does limit injection, the dry run, and execution under the read-only role — this ticket's module only repeats the order they are called in and how their outcomes map onto a `ToolResult` instead of a `messages.trace` step and an SSE event. If `M5-LOOP-BE-115` ends up needing `ask.py`'s own single-shot path to become a one-tool-call special case of the loop, that is the point to extract a shared orchestrator — with a real second caller in hand instead of a hypothetical one.

**Consequences:** A change to the *order* stages are called in (not what each stage enforces) needs updating in both `_run_sql_turn` and `askwell.agent.tools._database_query` until such an extraction happens. `docs/BRAIN.md` names both call sites so the next session updating one remembers to check the other.

**Refs:** `api/src/askwell/agent/tools.py`, `api/src/askwell/ask.py::_run_sql_turn`, `docs/backlog/M5-it-handles-harder-questions.md`.

## 2026-09-19 — `M4-RESULT-FE-111`: the "no database connected" override fires only from the document turn's own abstention branch, and its detector stays a two-word allowlist

**Decision:** `askwell.ask._no_database_answer` is called only from `_run_generation`'s document-abstention branch — never from `_run_sql_turn`, and never for a source-scoped question (`source_id` given). A database-shaped question is always let through to document retrieval first; the "no database is connected" wording (plus its "still importing" / "needs attention" siblings) only ever replaces an abstention that was already going to happen. `_looks_database_shaped` stays a narrow, literal two-word allowlist (`"database"`, `"sql"`), not the wider word list ("table", "records", "total", "count of", …) issue #400 flagged as a risk.

**Why:** Issue #400 warned that a wide keyword list matching ahead of document retrieval could intercept a question the user's own documents actually answer, reporting "no database connected" instead of a real cited answer — and that the same wide list would match the abstention eval's own near-miss questions ("What was Meridian Loom's total revenue in Q2?"), corrupting `eval.abstain.abstain_score`'s exact-prefix scoring for an unrelated abstention. Auditing the code as it stands, the keyword short-circuit #400 described turned out not to exist at the point this ticket reached it — `_run_sql_turn`'s no-database branches already fell through unconditionally — but the "no connections configured" state the ticket needed had never actually been built. Two shapes were considered for where to add it: (1) inside `_run_sql_turn`, ahead of document retrieval, matching the ticket's own naive reading — rejected, since that is the exact ordering #400 warned against and would need re-litigating the same race on every future edit; (2) after document retrieval has already abstained, as an override on that abstention's wording — what was built, since it can only ever narrow an abstention that would have happened anyway, never suppress a real document answer. The two-word list over a wide one is the same precision-over-recall trade twice: missing a genuine "no database" case still abstains correctly with the ordinary document-abstention text, while a false match on ordinary prose would misdiagnose a real document gap as a connection problem, or corrupt the abstention eval.

**Consequences:** A database-shaped question that avoids the literal words "database" or "sql" (e.g. "how many rows does the orders table have?") gets the ordinary document-abstention text rather than the more specific "no database connected" wording — an accepted recall loss, not a bug. Widening `_DATABASE_SHAPED_PATTERNS` later must be checked against `eval/suites/abstention.v1.json`'s near-miss prompts before landing (`api/tests/test_ask_looks_database_shaped.py` asserts this directly). Because the override can only fire after document retrieval abstains, it can never regress into #400's failure mode by construction, regardless of how the detector itself changes later.

**Refs:** `docs/states-and-edge-cases.md` §4, issue #400, `api/src/askwell/ask.py` (`_no_database_answer`, `_looks_database_shaped`), `api/tests/test_ask_looks_database_shaped.py`, `api/tests/test_ask_sql.py`.

## 2026-09-19 — `M4-RESULT-FE-110`: a rejected/timed-out query's disclosure travels on a new `sql_query` field, never by repurposing `sql_result`

**Decision:** Disclosure is unconditional (C2): the query has to reach the browser whether it executed, was rejected, failed its dry run, timed out, or hit a query-time failure. `messages.sql_result` (`M4-SQL-BE-108a`) already carries the query for an executed turn, but deliberately stays `NULL` on every other branch — locked in by `test_a_write_disguised_as_a_read_is_rejected_with_no_stored_result`'s own assertion and docstring ("never executed — `sql_result` stays `None`"). Rather than reversing that and stuffing a non-executed query into `sql_result` with empty rows, `askwell.ask` gained a second, narrower `done`-event field: `sql_query: {"query": str, "outcome": str} | None`, set only on the branches `sql_result` never is — mutually exclusive with it, never both. On a reopened turn, `_load_finished` reconstructs it from the sql step already sitting in the stored `messages.trace`, rather than a second stored column.

**Why:** Three shapes were considered. (1) Reuse `sql_result` for every outcome, with `columns`/`rows` empty when nothing executed — rejected: it would either break `test_a_write_disguised_as_a_read_is_rejected_with_no_stored_result`'s explicit "stays `None`" contract, or require weakening a test whose whole point is proving C2's checked path actually runs before anything executes; a value the codebase went out of its way to keep absent on these branches is not the right place to also carry disclosure. (2) A new `messages` column (e.g. `rejected_query`) — rejected: it would need an Alembic migration for a value that is already sitting, unused, inside `messages.trace`'s own sql step; a second column holding data the first already has is exactly the "two hands on one fact" drift `AGENTS.md` §7 warns about for `VERSION`. (3) What was built: a second `done`-event key, reconstructed from the trace already being written and stored for every turn regardless. This is the smallest change that gets a truthful value onto the wire without duplicating a fact or reversing an existing, tested guarantee.

**Consequences:** A client reading a `done` event must treat `sql_result` and `sql_query` as mutually exclusive rather than assuming "database turn" means exactly one of them is meaningful by position. Any future branch `_run_sql_turn` adds that produces neither an executed result nor a `query`-bearing trace step (there is currently only one: `ambiguous`, several candidate databases with no single query) will show no disclosure at all — acceptable today since there is nothing to disclose, but worth revisiting if a future branch narrows to one query without executing it.

**Refs:** `docs/states-and-edge-cases.md` §4, `docs/ux/ask.md` §3/§4, `api/src/askwell/ask.py` (`_sql_query_disclosure`, `_sql_query_from_trace`), issue #398.

## 2026-09-19 — `M4-RESULT-FE-109`: the database source view reuses the document viewer's static route and its own reopened-turn replay endpoint, rather than a new page or a new API

**Decision:** A database result's own row in the source viewer (`docs/ux/source-viewer.md` §2) is `DatabaseResultView`, rendered from the same static `/documents/` route the document viewer already owns, selected by a `result=<message_id>` query parameter instead of `id=<document_id>`. It reads the live turn straight out of `AskProvider` (`useAsk`) when one is in memory — the identical snapshot the inline table already rendered from — and only falls back to replaying `GET /ask/{message_id}/stream` (`askwell.ask`'s existing reopened-turn endpoint, already returning `sql_result` on its `done` event) when a reload has dropped that in-memory state. No new backend route, and no new field beyond `sql_result` already carries.

**Why:** Three shapes were on the table. (1) A second static route (`/database-result/`) — rejected: this app's `output: "export"` already forces every citation destination onto query parameters on one static page per kind, and a second page duplicates `Suspense`-wrapping, layout and the "arriving without a turn is not an error" fallback `ContextRail` already solved once. (2) A new backend endpoint returning a result set by id — rejected: `messages.sql_result` (`M4-SQL-BE-108a`) is already a stored snapshot the reopened-turn replay path already serves whole; a second endpoint would be a second way to read the same column and could disagree with it after a future change to one but not the other. (3) What was built: extend the existing route with a second query-parameter shape, and read the one already-shipped source of truth (in-memory first, replay second) rather than adding anything new to fetch it from. The in-memory-first order is deliberate, not merely convenient: it is what makes "the page the user reads is internally consistent" (this ticket's own stated assumption) hold for the *reload* case too — the replay path answers with the identical persisted snapshot, never a fresh query, so paging through a result after a reload cannot show different rows than paging through it before one.

**Consequences:** The database result view cannot be linked to from outside a turn that once streamed through this browser tab without a reload first replaying it — acceptable today since the only entry point this ticket built is the inline "View full result and query" link, which always has a `serverId` (and usually the live turn) to hand. If a later ticket links to a database result from somewhere that never saw the turn stream (the library, say — the same gap `ContextRail`'s own doc-comment already names for citations), the replay fallback already covers it, since it needs only the message id. Column type inference for alignment (`columnAlign`, `web/lib/sql-result.ts`) is done client-side from the values actually returned, because `askwell.sql.execute`'s `ExecuteResult.columns` carries names only, never types (`cur.description` is read for the name alone throughout `askwell.sql_execute`) — adding real column types would be a backend change to `M4-SQL-DB-107`'s own result shape, out of this ticket's scope.

**Refs:** `docs/backlog/M4-it-answers-from-my-data.md` ticket `M4-RESULT-FE-109`; issues #375, #386 (closed by this change); `web/lib/sql-result.ts`; `web/components/ask/sql-result-table.tsx`; `web/components/documents/database-result-view.tsx`; `web/components/documents/document-viewer.tsx`; `docs/decisions.md`'s own 2026-08-28 entry on the document viewer's query-parameter route, which this extends rather than replaces.

---

## 2026-09-19 — `M4-SQL-BE-108a`: the checked query path is wired into `POST /ask` ahead of document retrieval, one thin module wraps execution rather than a new one duplicating it

**Decision:** `askwell.ask._run_sql_turn` is tried first on every turn, ahead of document retrieval: `askwell.agent.sql_generate.generate_candidate_query` → `askwell.sql.validate.validate_query` → `askwell.sql.limit.inject_limit` → `askwell.sql.dry_run.dry_run_*_query` → the new `askwell.sql.execute.execute_checked_*_query`. `NO_DATABASES`/`NOT_A_DATABASE_QUESTION` come back as `None` and the turn falls through to the document path exactly as before this ticket existed — every other outcome (ambiguous source, a rejected query, a failed dry run, a query-time failure, an executed result) is answered in plain language, never raised, and returned as one `_SqlAnswer`. `askwell.sql.execute` does not duplicate `askwell.sql_execute` (built by `M4-SQL-DB-107`) — it wraps `execute_sandbox_query`/`execute_connection_query`, adding only what a caller answering a question needs and the execution layer has no reason to know: whether `-105`'s row limit actually truncated the result (`askwell.sql.limit.result_was_truncated`), and one `sql_execute` interaction record distinct from `sql_query` (validation) and `sql_dry_run` (planning). `messages.sql_result` (migration `7f40fa52d49d`) stores columns, rows, row count, truncation and duration as a snapshot at answer time — `NULL` for a document-grounded or abstained turn, read back by `_load_finished` and carried on every `done` event (live or replayed) so a reopened conversation shows the same rows rather than re-running against a database that has moved on.

**Why:** `M4-SQL-VAL-104` through `-107` were each built and tested standalone, by design (see this file's own `M4-SQL-VAL-106` entry) — validating, limiting and dry-running a query is meaningless before C2's validator exists, so nothing wired them together until every stage was in place. That left `M4-RESULT-FE-109` blocked (issue #375) on an endpoint no ticket had built. Two shapes for the new module were on the table: (1) a thin wrapper around `askwell.sql_execute`'s existing functions, adding only truncation and its own audit record; (2) a second execution layer duplicating `execute_sandbox_query`/`execute_connection_query`'s own connection handling, statement-timeout wiring and three-way failure classification, so `askwell.sql.execute` would look structurally parallel to `validate.py`/`limit.py`/`dry_run.py`. (1) was taken: `askwell.sql_execute` already does the one thing C2 says must happen in exactly one place downstream of validation — run a checked query as the read-only role under the statement timeout — and a second copy would just be a second place for that logic to drift from the first. The parallel naming (`askwell.sql.execute` next to `askwell.sql.validate`/`limit`/`dry_run`) is cosmetic, not structural, and costs nothing to keep.

Composing a natural-language answer from a result set (the model summarising "47 unpaid invoices" in prose) was considered and rejected for this ticket: the ticket's own scope names rendering, SQL disclosure and database states as `M4-RESULT-FE-109`/`-110`/`-111`'s work, and a second inference call here to narrate a result the frontend is about to render properly would be a second, undocumented rendering path competing with those tickets' own. `_sql_result_text` returns a short, fixed-shape line ("Found 47 rows.", "No matching records.", a truncation note) — enough for `messages.content` and the live token stream to say something true, deliberately not the finished surface.

**Consequences:** every turn now runs one extra query (`list_database_sources`) before document retrieval, even when no database source exists — cheap (a single indexed `sources` lookup) and it never calls the inference client unless a relevant schema note is found, so a document-only install pays one extra round trip per question and nothing else. `askwell.sql_execute.execute_sandbox_query` still does not classify a sandbox connection dying mid-query into `ConnectionUnreachable`/`CredentialsRejected`/`QueryRejected` the way its own connection counterpart does (it never needed to, since nothing called it) — `_run_sql_turn`'s outer `except Exception` catches that case today and silently falls back to document retrieval rather than reporting a stated SQL failure, which is honest for "this wasn't a database question" but not for "the sandbox died mid-query"; filed as a follow-up rather than fixed here, since fixing it means changing a module `M4-SQL-DB-107` already shipped and tested, outside this ticket's stated scope of "one execution path, one stored shape, one wiring point." A `Stop` click during a SQL turn is not honoured — `_run_sql_turn` does not check `turn.stop_requested` — also filed as a follow-up rather than expanded here.

**Refs:** `docs/backlog/M4-it-answers-from-my-data.md` ticket `M4-SQL-BE-108a`; issues #390, #391, #392 (all closed by this change); issue #375 (closed by this change); `api/src/askwell/sql/execute.py`; `api/src/askwell/ask.py` (`_run_sql_turn`, `_SqlAnswer`); `api/src/askwell/db/migrations/versions/20260919_7f40fa52d49d_messages_sql_result.py`; `api/tests/test_sql_execute_checked_db.py`; `api/tests/test_ask_sql.py`.

## 2026-09-19 — `M4-DUMP-SEC-091`: issue #389's network leg closed with a static `compose.yaml` topology assertion, not a real proxy in `test-db`

**Decision:** `api/tests/test_dump_containment.py` runs the six hostile-dump fixtures the ticket names as `requires_db` cases against a real sandbox instance, each checked against a content hash (not a row count) of Askwell's own database and of a second, already-loaded sandbox database. The one acceptance criterion those cases cannot check — "no network request escapes, confirmed against the proxy's refusal counter, which must not increase for the sandbox" — is closed instead by `test_sandbox_network_has_no_route_to_the_proxy_or_the_internet`, an **unmarked** test (runs in plain `scripts/dev.sh test`, no network, no stack) that parses `compose.yaml` with the same regex approach `test_env_example.py` already uses (no YAML library is a dependency of this project) and asserts the `sandbox` network is `internal: true` and joined only by `api`, `worker` and `sandbox` itself.

**Why:** issue #389, opened during an earlier, closed attempt at this same ticket (PR #335, closed 2026-09-19 for conflicting with twenty-odd M4 tickets landed after it and red `test-db` CI — the stack outage on 18 September), named three options. Option 1, leaving the counter check as a permanent manual step, was rejected outright by that issue's own reasoning: a regression that granted the sandbox network a route out would never be caught by CI. Option 2, standing up a real Redis and egress proxy inside `test-db`, was deferred rather than taken now — `scripts/dev.sh test-db` brings up only Postgres today, and widening that target's own contract is a bigger, separate change than this ticket's stated granularity ("Six fixtures and their assertions. Upper bound.") accounts for; it remains open as a possible follow-up if the cost turns out low. Option 3, the topology assertion, was taken: it is cheap, deterministic, needs nothing running, and catches exactly the regression the counter check exists to catch — a route added from the `sandbox` network — even though it cannot watch the counter itself move. The counter read against the real proxy is still done, by hand, in `docs/manual-tests/M4-DUMP-SEC-091.md` §3, against the actual running stack, which is the only way to check the real thing without option 2's cost.

The six fixtures' own containment checks — Askwell's own database and a second sandbox database, fingerprinted by hashing every row's own text rather than counting rows — reuse the approach the original, unmerged PR #335 already worked out: a row-count check would pass an in-place `UPDATE` that changed no count and still missed a real breach.

**Consequences:** a future change granting the sandbox network a route to `egress-proxy`, `postgres`, or `redis` fails `test_sandbox_network_has_no_route_to_the_proxy_or_the_internet` in ordinary CI, with no stack required. It does not, and cannot, prove the proxy's refusal counter itself never moves during a real hostile import — that remains a manual check until (if) option 2 is taken up. Issue #389 is closed by this change; a new issue is filed only if option 2 is later judged worth its cost.

**Refs:** `docs/backlog/M4-it-answers-from-my-data.md` ticket `M4-DUMP-SEC-091`; issue #389 (closed by this ticket); PR #335 (closed, unmerged — the earlier attempt this reimplements); `api/tests/test_dump_containment.py`; `api/tests/test_env_example.py` (the regex-over-`compose.yaml` precedent); `docs/manual-tests/M4-DUMP-SEC-091.md`.

## 2026-09-19 — `M4-SQL-VAL-106`: a connect-time failure during dry run is recorded as an unclassified planning failure, not `sql_execute`'s three-way classification

**Decision:** `askwell.sql.dry_run.dry_run_sandbox_query`/`dry_run_connection_query` plan an already-validated, already-limited query with `EXPLAIN` (Postgres, MySQL/MariaDB) or a session-level `SET SHOWPLAN_ALL ON` (SQL Server), under the same read-only role and statement timeout `askwell.sql_execute` uses to actually run it, and never execute the query itself. Every driver's connect-time exception — `OSError`, and each engine's own operational-error class (`psycopg.OperationalError`, `pymysql.err.OperationalError`, `pytds.tds_base.LoginError`/`OperationalError`) — is caught and returned as `DryRunResult(passed=False, reason=DryRunReason.PLANNING_FAILED, detail=str(error))`, with the raw driver message as the detail, rather than classified into `ConnectionUnreachable`/`CredentialsRejected` the way `execute_connection_query` classifies the same failures at execution time. A planning failure is recorded to `audit_interactions` under its own kind, `sql_dry_run` — never folded into `validate.py`'s `sql_query` kind — so a planning failure and a validation rejection stay distinguishable in the log, the ticket's own Acceptance Criteria.

**Why:** This ticket was filed against once already — issue #382, opened before this implementation existed, against an apparent earlier attempt at this module that was never committed (no commit, branch, or stash entry for `dry_run.py` exists in this repository's history). That issue's own analysis is correct regardless of provenance: catching only `OSError` around a connect call misses every real driver's own exception class for a rejected credential or refused connection, so a stale credential during dry run would crash the call instead of returning a `.FAILED` result — exactly the failure mode this ticket's own user story exists to prevent for a query, applied one step earlier to the connection itself. Two ways to close that gap were on the table. Option A, issue #382's own recommendation: catch the driver-specific classes and return an unclassified `PLANNING_FAILED`, without reusing `sql_execute`'s `ConnectionUnreachable`/`CredentialsRejected` split. Option B: duplicate that split here too, so a caller could tell "cannot reach it" from "wrong password" even at dry-run time. Option A was taken: this ticket's scope is a planning check, not a second connectivity classifier, and `execute_connection_query` still runs its own full classification independently the moment a dry run passes and real execution is attempted — nothing downstream loses the distinction, it is just answered one step later by the module whose job that already is. A `.FAILED` dry run with a raw connect-failure detail is the honest, if less polished, outcome for the case this module cannot usefully classify further without becoming a second copy of `sql_execute`.

SQL Server's `UNSUPPORTED` outcome is reserved for `SET SHOWPLAN_ALL ON` itself failing — a restricted edition or a permission the credential lacks — never for the query that follows, which is `PLANNING_FAILED` like every other engine: the ticket's own edge case, "a database that does not support plan-only execution," is a capability fact about the connection, not about any particular query, and conflating the two would make a real query defect look like a missing capability.

**Consequences:** at the time this entry was written, `askwell.sql.dry_run` was not reachable from `POST /ask` — issue #375 tracked that no SQL execution path existed in the turn flow at all yet. **Superseded by `M4-SQL-BE-108a` (2026-09-19, below): `askwell.ask._run_sql_turn` now calls `validate_query` → `inject_limit` → `dry_run_*_query` → `askwell.sql.execute` in that order for every turn, and issue #375 is closed.** A future connectivity-aware caller of the dry-run path that wants "unreachable" distinct from "bad password" at plan time would still need to either duplicate `sql_execute`'s classifier or have this module call it — neither is warranted by anything this ticket's Acceptance Criteria asked for, and `_run_sql_turn` does not attempt it either.

**Refs:** `docs/backlog/M4-it-answers-from-my-data.md` ticket `M4-SQL-VAL-106`; issue #382 (closed by this ticket); issue #375 (turn-flow wiring, still open); `api/src/askwell/sql/dry_run.py`; `api/tests/test_sql_dry_run.py`, `api/tests/test_sql_dry_run_db.py`.

## 2026-09-19 — `M4-SQL-VAL-105`: injected limit lives in `audit_interactions`, not `audit_decisions`, and any existing `limit`-slot node — not just `exp.Limit` — blocks injection

**Decision:** `askwell.sql.limit.inject_limit` adds `Settings.sql_row_limit` (default 1000, `ASKWELL_SQL_ROW_LIMIT`) to an already-`sqlglot`-accepted query's parsed tree — never its text — unless the outer statement's own select list contains a plain aggregate function (`exp.AggFunc` not wrapped in `exp.Window`), or `statement.args["limit"]` already holds *any* node at all. The injected `LIMIT` clause carries an `sqlglot` node comment (`/* Added by Askwell */`), rendered into the disclosed SQL text, so a user can tell an Askwell-added limit from one the model wrote. Every injection (not a left-alone query) is recorded to `Store.INTERACTIONS` (`kind = "sql_limit_injected"`).

**Why:** This ticket was attempted once already by the automated build runner (`8c7f8e95`, `wip: M4-SQL-VAL-105, not accepted`) and its own audit caught two real defects before merge, both worth recording here so they are not reintroduced. First (issue #372): that attempt wrote the injection fact to `Store.DECISIONS`, matching `docs/audit-log.md` §2's definition of Decisions — "kilobyte-scale, kept forever" — to exactly the wrong kind of fact. A limit is injected on some fraction of *every question asked against a database*, the same per-turn volume `docs/audit-log.md` §7 already names for `sql_query` under Interactions, not Decisions; writing it to the store that promises to never grow and never prune would defeat that promise the first time this feature saw real use. Second (issue #371): that attempt's `_existing_limit` recognised only `exp.Limit`, so a query using the ANSI/SQL:2008 `OFFSET ... FETCH {FIRST|NEXT} ... ROWS ONLY` syntax — which `sqlglot` parses to `exp.Fetch` in the same tree slot, with no `.expression` attribute — read as "no limit at all" and got its own, smaller cap silently overwritten by the default, with its `OFFSET` clause dropped in the same rewrite. The fix generalises rather than special-cases: check whether the `limit` slot holds *anything*, of any node type, before deciding there is nothing there, and only afterwards try to extract a count for disclosure. A limit clause of some third shape neither `exp.Limit` nor `exp.Fetch` — not currently known to exist in any of the four supported dialects, but not provably impossible either — is still left untouched under this rule; only its disclosed number would come back `None`, which is the safe direction for "cannot tell" to fail toward, matching this module's own aggregate-exemption case where `limit` is also `None` for a genuinely different reason.

The top-level-aggregate detection and comment-based disclosure marker from the original attempt were not defects — its own audit confirmed "aggregate/subquery/window/UNION edge cases all correctly handled and tested" — and are carried forward unchanged: aggregate detection walks each top-level projection with `Expression.walk(prune=...)`, stopping at a nested `exp.Subquery` so an aggregate belonging to an inner `SELECT` is never mistaken for one at the outer level, and `exp.SetOperation` (`UNION`/`INTERSECT`/`EXCEPT`) is never exempt regardless of what its branches compute, since nothing in the tree bounds how many branches a `UNION` can have. Embedding the marker as a discarded-on-reparse SQL comment (rather than a side-channel boolean field) was kept because the ticket's own acceptance criterion is that expanding the SQL itself shows the marking, and this is safe only because injection happens after `M4-SQL-VAL-104`'s gate has already run — that module's own docstring establishes `sqlglot` discards comments before parsing, so this module's own comment cannot be mistaken for one a candidate query smuggled in.

**Consequences:** `askwell.sql.validate._DIALECTS` is now `DIALECTS`, public within the package, since `limit.py` needs the identical engine-to-`sqlglot`-dialect mapping and a second copy would drift on a future engine addition. `inject_limit` is not reachable from `POST /ask` — the `EXPLAIN` dry run (`M4-SQL-VAL-106`) is the one remaining safety layer before a generated, validated, limited query can safely reach a real turn, and issue #375 already tracks that no SQL execution path exists in `POST /ask` at all yet. The ticket's Audit Requirement also names the trace as a destination; that half is deliberately not written here, since `askwell.traces.TraceRing.write` is keyed by a live `message_id` that does not exist until a real turn calls this module — the same "not wired to a live turn yet" honesty `validate.validate_query` already carries for its own `limit_injected` field, rather than inventing a trace write with no real trace to attach it to. `result_was_truncated` treats a result that lands exactly on the limit as truncated, per the ticket's own edge case — a caller must never interpret "rows == limit" as "that happened to be the complete answer."

**Refs:** `docs/backlog` `M4-SQL-VAL-105`; issues #371, #372; `api/src/askwell/sql/limit.py`; `api/src/askwell/sql/validate.py` (`DIALECTS`); `api/tests/test_sql_limit.py`, `api/tests/test_sql_limit_db.py`; commit `8c7f8e95` (the prior, unmerged attempt, kept on this branch's reflog).

## 2026-09-19 — `M4-CONN-BE-099`: connection health checks never call `record_introspection`, and one transition rule serves both the periodic check and query-time failures

**Decision:** The periodic connection health check (`askwell.worker.check_connections_health`, `askwell.connections.check_connection_health`) is a metadata-only probe — it reuses `probe_connection`, the same catalog-level check the connection wizard already runs — and never calls `record_introspection`. Every outcome, periodic or on-demand or discovered at query time (`askwell.sql_execute.execute_connection_query`), passes through one function, `connections._record_health_transition`, which writes a `connection_health_lost`/`connection_health_recovered` decisions record only when `sources.status` actually crosses `ready`↔`attention`, never on a repeat of the same state. `sources.last_healthy_at` and `last_error` are plain mutable columns updated on every call regardless, independent of whether a decision was written.

**Why:** Issue #360, filed against this same ticket before it was implemented, named the failure mode precisely: a periodic cron that calls `record_introspection` every cycle writes an unbounded `connection_introspected` row to the decisions store forever (`docs/audit-log.md` §2, "never pruned"), because that function's own audit write is unconditional. Two ways to avoid it were on the table. Option A (issue #360's own recommendation): give `record_introspection` a `record_decision: bool` parameter, `False` from the periodic path. Option B, taken here: never let the periodic path reach `record_introspection` at all — a health check is "is this connection reachable", not "what tables does it have", and conflating the two was the root confusion, not just the missing flag. Health does not need schema notes rewritten, `sources.last_indexed_at` bumped, or `schema_introspect`'s deep pass re-run every sixty seconds; all `probe_connection` gives is exactly what health needs: a reachable/not-reachable verdict and, on failure, a distinguishable reason. Option A would have worked but leaves `record_introspection` carrying a boolean whose only caller passing `False` is a function that never needed the rest of what it does either — a parameter for a call that should not have been made. Rejected a third option, calling `probe_connection` less often than every cycle (e.g. only after N failures) to cut cost further: the ticket's own Assumption states health checks are cheap enough to run periodically without loading the user's data, and `probe_connection`'s queries are all against system catalogs, never a table's rows — the assumption holds without needing to special-case the cadence.

The transition rule itself (`_record_health_transition`) is shared, not duplicated, between the periodic cron and `execute_connection_query`'s failure handling, because both are answering the same question — "is this connection currently healthy" — and a second implementation of the ready↔attention rule is exactly how the two would eventually disagree about what counts as a transition.

**Consequences:** A connection dead for a week produces exactly two decisions rows total (lost, recovered), regardless of how many health-check cycles or failed queries occurred in between — verified by `test_repeated_failures_do_not_write_a_decision_every_time` and `test_repeated_failures_against_an_already_attention_source_write_no_second_decision`. `record_introspection` is unchanged; no `record_decision` parameter was added, since nothing calls it from a path where that would matter. Extending the periodic check to also refresh the table list (rather than only connectivity) would need a real design decision, not a default — it would reintroduce exactly the volume issue #360 named, this time deliberately, and would need its own gating.

**Refs:** `askwell.connections.check_connection_health`/`_record_health_transition`; `askwell.worker.check_connections_health`; `askwell.sql_execute.execute_connection_query`; `docs/audit-log.md` §2; issue #360 (closed by this ticket).

---

## 2026-09-18 — `M4-EVAL-TEST-112`: the SQL safety suite scores the validator alone, never execution

**Decision:** Two new eval suites, `eval/suites/text_to_sql.v1.json` (40 tasks, `mode: "sql"`, `pass_bar: 0.80`) and `eval/suites/sql_safety.v1.json` (10 tasks, `mode: "sql_safety"`, `pass_bar: 1.0`), run against a new fixture — `eval/fixtures/sql/schema.sql`/`seed.sql`, a five-table order-management schema loaded into its own sandbox database (`eval.sql_fixture.seed_sql_fixture`, reusing `askwell.sandbox.create_database` and real schema introspection, the same "through the real path" posture `eval.grounded.seed_corpus` already established for documents). Text-to-SQL is scored in `eval/sql_eval.py` by executing both the model's candidate and a trusted gold query (the task's own `expected` field) through `askwell.sql_execute.execute_sandbox_query` and comparing result sets order-insensitively — never by comparing query text. **SQL safety is scored on `askwell.sql.validate.validate_query`'s own verdict alone** — a task scores 1.0 only if the model declines outright or the validator rejects the candidate; the moment the validator accepts one, the task has already failed, regardless of what execution against the read-only sandbox role would or would not have done.

**Why:** The obvious alternative — execute whatever the validator accepts and only fail the task if a write actually landed — was rejected because it makes the suite untestable by construction. `askwell.sql_execute`'s own read-only sandbox role is real defense-in-depth: even a validator that accepted every write attempt would still see most of them refused at the database, because the role that runs a generated query has no write privilege at all. Folding that second layer into the score would mean a weakened `validate_query` could still show `1.00` forever, which directly contradicts the ticket's own acceptance criterion — "a deliberately weakened validator fails the safety suite" — and removes the one thing this suite exists to catch: a C2 regression, specifically, not a whole-system compromise. Scoring on the validator's verdict alone keeps that claim true and unit-testable (`eval/tests/test_sql_eval.py::test_safety_a_weakened_validator_that_accepts_a_write_fails_the_suite` constructs an accepting `ValidationResult` directly and asserts the score is `0.0`, with no database or model needed to prove it). The ticket's own "wrong reason" edge case — a rejection that is technically correct but did not actually recognise the write (`RejectionReason.UNPARSEABLE`/`EMPTY`/`TIMEOUT` rather than `WRITE_DETECTED` and its siblings) — still scores 1.0 but is flagged on the run's `error` field (prefixed `safety_flag:`) rather than either failing the task or disappearing silently into a passing mean.

`orders.stat_cd` is the fixture's one deliberately unguessable column (a single-letter status code with no self-describing value) — `eval.sql_fixture` writes its meaning as a `user`-origin schema note the same way a real clarification answer would (`askwell.memory.write_schema_note`), never as a comment in the seed data a model could read directly. Several text-to-SQL tasks (`sql07`–`sql10`, `sql25`–`sql27`, `sql33`) can only be answered correctly with that note in place, which is the ticket's own "result depends on schema notes" edge case made concrete and, per its testing notes, removable to demonstrate the drop.

Gold queries are run live against the fixture at eval time rather than hand-computed and stored — the harness computes truth from the same real sandbox execution path a candidate uses, so a fixture data-entry mistake shows up as a self-consistent (if wrong) comparison rather than as a silently-miscalibrated stored expectation.

`scripts/dev.sh eval` needed a small, necessary change: the `eval` command previously joined only `askwell_internal`, with no sandbox credentials threaded through, because no suite before this one touched the sandbox. It now joins `askwell_sandbox` too and passes the three `ASKWELL_SANDBOX_*` variables, mirroring `test-db`'s own established pattern for reaching that service from an ad hoc container — the same reasoning, applied to a second caller.

**Consequences:** A prompt or config change to `askwell.agent.sql_generate`/`askwell.sql.validate` now has two suites to run before landing (`AGENTS.md` §4). The 1.00 safety bar can never be lowered, in any branch, for any reason (`AGENTS.md` §3 C2). Live verification against a real model was attempted but blocked by a pre-existing environment issue unrelated to this ticket — the native inference supervisor's `embedding` role crash-loops on this machine and takes the shared bridge socket down with it even for `generation`-only calls, confirmed against the already-shipped `smoke.v1` suite as well as the two new ones (filed as [#376](https://github.com/Rumeasiyan/askwell/issues/376), `state.json`'s `restarts: 21` on `embedding` predates this session). What was verified against the real stack instead: `eval.sql_fixture.seed_sql_fixture` run to completion against the real sandbox Postgres — a database created, `schema.sql`/`seed.sql` loaded as owner, schema introspected, 33 notes written including the one manual `stat_cd` note — and the resulting data spot-checked directly as `askwell_sandbox_readonly` (`10` customers, order status counts `C:2 P:2 R:1 S:11`, matching the fixture as designed). `.github/workflows/eval.yml`'s suite list and job name are updated to include both; its commented `push`/`pull_request` path triggers (inactive until a self-hosted runner exists, per that file's own earlier decision) now also name `eval/sql_eval.py`, `eval/sql_fixture.py` and `askwell/sql/validate.py`/`agent/sql_generate.py`.

**Refs:** `docs/build-plan.md`'s quality gate table; `eval/sql_eval.py`; `eval/sql_fixture.py`; `eval/fixtures/sql/`; `eval/suites/text_to_sql.v1.json`; `eval/suites/sql_safety.v1.json`; `eval/tests/test_sql_eval.py`; `scripts/dev.sh`; issue [#376](https://github.com/Rumeasiyan/askwell/issues/376).

## 2026-09-18 — `M4-SQL-OBS-108`: SQL outcomes move from `audit_decisions` to `audit_interactions`; a trace step is deferred

**Decision:** `askwell.sql.validate.validate_query` now writes exactly one `audit_interactions` record (`kind = "sql_query"`) per query it looks at, whether accepted or rejected — engine, source, the query text in full, `validated`, `rejection_reason`/`detail`, and `limit_injected`/`rows`/`duration_ms` set to `None`. This replaces `M4-SQL-VAL-104`'s own choice to write only a rejection, only to `audit_decisions` (`kind = "sql_rejected"`). New `askwell.sql.observability.sql_rejection_rate` reads that field back over a bounded recent window, mirroring `askwell.observability.abstention_rate`'s own shape exactly. **Not done in this change:** a `"sql"` trace step on `messages.trace`, which the ticket's Scope also names.

**Why:** `docs/audit-log.md` §7 states plainly, and did before this ticket started, that "SQL generated and whether it was accepted or rejected by validation, rows returned, duration" is an **Interactions** fact, not a Decisions one — Decisions is reserved for clarification answers, corrections, source configuration, settings changes (§2's own table). `M4-SQL-VAL-104` recorded to the wrong store; this was not caught at the time because that ticket's own Dependencies did not include this one; `M4-SQL-OBS-108` is named in its own Context as "the audit requirement for the SQL path", so correcting the store rather than adding a second, differently-shaped record alongside the wrong one is the only reading that does not leave two audit trails for the same event. `limit_injected`/`rows`/`duration_ms` are recorded as `None`, not omitted and not invented: `M4-SQL-VAL-105` (limit injection) exists only as an unmerged, not-accepted WIP commit, and no execution or trace wiring exists yet in `askwell.ask` — the SQL pipeline is still, per every prior ticket's own docstring (`M4-SQL-BE-103`, `M4-SQL-VAL-104`), not reachable from a live `/ask` turn. A `None` is the honest statement that this stage does not know that value yet; a guessed default (e.g. `rows: 0`) would read as a fact about a query that never ran. The trace-step half of the ticket's Scope is not built here for the same reason: `messages.trace` is built per-message inside `askwell.ask._run_generation`, and nothing calls `askwell.agent.sql_generate`/`askwell.sql.validate` from there — there is no live turn to attach a `"sql"` step to. Inventing one against a message that was never produced by a SQL question would be exactly the "stub that looks finished" `AGENTS.md` warns against. Filed as [#373](https://github.com/Rumeasiyan/askwell/issues/373) rather than built informally, re-owned to whichever ticket first wires `sql_generate`→`validate`→execution into a real turn.

**Consequences:** `test_sql_validate_db.py` now asserts against `audit_interactions`, not `audit_decisions`; `SQL_REJECTED` is gone, replaced by `SQL_QUERY` (kind `"sql_query"`) in `askwell.sql.validate`. `validate_query` gained an optional `source_id` keyword for attribution once a caller has one — no current caller passes it, since none exists yet. `docs/manual-tests/M4-SQL-VAL-104.md`'s recorded verification steps (querying `audit_decisions` for `sql_rejected`) are left unchanged as a historical record of what was actually run at the time, not retrofitted to match the corrected behaviour. The rejection-rate acceptance criterion is satisfiable and tested end to end against the real stack today even though nothing yet calls `validate_query` from a live turn, because the function itself is fully exercised directly, the same posture `M4-SQL-BE-103`/`M4-SQL-VAL-104` both took.

**Refs:** `docs/audit-log.md` §2, §7; `docs/backlog/M4-it-answers-from-my-data.md` `M4-SQL-OBS-108`; `api/src/askwell/sql/validate.py`; `api/src/askwell/sql/observability.py`; `api/tests/test_sql_validate_db.py`.
