## 2026-09-18 — `M3-APPLY-BE-079`: a memory fact is cited by continuing the document index space, and `fact_usage` stays message-scoped, not claim-scoped

**Decision:** A retrieved memory fact or schema note gets a citation index of its own — `compose_conflict`'s `_delimit_memory_facts`/`_delimit_schema_notes` number entries starting right after the last document candidate (`facts_start = len(candidates) + 1`, `notes_start = facts_start + len(retrieved_facts)`), rather than inventing a second marker syntax like `[F1]`. `ask._cite_claim` resolves an index in that range against `fact_usage` (one row per `(message_id, fact_kind, fact_id)`, de-duplicated in Python before the insert) instead of `citations`, and emits a new `fact_citation` SSE event carrying the fact's own text, origin and `created_at` as the attribution date. `fact_usage` itself gained no `claim_ordinal` column — the table already existed, built ahead of time by `M3-STORE-BE-076`'s own migration, with the shape "which facts did this message use," not "which claim used which fact."

**Why:** One index space means the prompt teaches the model one citation convention — "refer to it by the number in front of it" — rather than two, and it reuses `_CLAIM_RE`/`_MARKER_RE` in `askwell.agent.claims` completely unchanged, since `[7]` parses identically regardless of what it resolves to. The alternative — a second bracket syntax for memory — was rejected as needless: nothing about a citation *marker* needs to say what kind of thing it points at; that only matters at resolution time, which is exactly where `_cite_claim` already branches on which candidate the index fell in. Not adding `claim_ordinal` to `fact_usage` was accepted deliberately rather than extending the migration: `askwell.agent.citation_check.check_citations` already excludes (does not flag) a whole message carrying any `fact_usage` row, a decision `M3-APPLY-RET-078`'s session made in anticipation of this ticket precisely because per-claim fact attribution was not yet real. Revisiting that to make the uncited-claim check claim-exact for a mixed document/fact answer is real future work, but it is a second migration and a second read path for a distinction ("did this specific sentence go uncited") the ticket's own Effort estimate (3–4 hours) did not budget for, and the coarser message-level exclusion already satisfies its literal Acceptance Criteria ("the uncited-claim check counts a fact-backed claim as cited").

**Consequences:** A message that mixes a genuinely uncited document claim with an unrelated fact-backed claim is invisible to `check_citations` today — it is wholly excluded rather than flagged for the one real gap. Reversing this means adding `claim_ordinal` to `fact_usage` (a migration) and rewriting `check_citations` to check per-ordinal across two tables instead of one exclusion set.

**Refs:** `api/src/askwell/agent/conflict.py`, `api/src/askwell/ask.py` (`_cite_claim`), `api/src/askwell/agent/citation_check.py`, `api/src/askwell/db/migrations/versions/20260827_a8208099ef38_v1_schema.py` (`fact_usage`, unchanged), issue #310.

---

## 2026-09-18 — `M3-APPLY-RET-078`: memory retrieval stays lexical full-text for now, and is a second, separate hook alongside `compose_conflict`'s existing `memory_fact` parameter rather than a replacement for it

**Decision:** `askwell.memory.retrieve_relevant_facts` ranks and bounds active `memory` facts and `schema_notes` against a question using Postgres full-text search (`plainto_tsquery`, with its default `&` turned into `|` so any matching term counts, not every one), not embedding similarity. `compose_conflict` gained `retrieved_facts`/`retrieved_notes` as new parameters, delimited into their own `<memory-facts>`/`<schema-notes>` blocks, left entirely separate from the existing `memory_fact` string parameter `M3-INLINE-FE-085` built for the "a just-answered clarification resolves this exact conflict" case.

**Why:** `docs/memory-and-clarification.md` line 70 describes embedding as the eventual retrieval mechanism, and the ticket's own Assumption line said facts "are embedded on write" — neither is true today: `memory` has no `embedding` column, and `schema_notes.embedding` exists but nothing populates it (issue #292, filed against this same gap before this session started implementing the ticket). Building the embedding path now — a migration, wiring `InferenceClient.embed()` into both write functions, switching this retrieval to cosine similarity — was rejected as disproportionate to what the ticket actually asks for ("one retrieval addition, one prompt change", per its own Granularity note) and to what the product's scale needs today: a single user's own small, personal memory store, where full-text over a few words of fact text is a reasonable interim rather than a silent downgrade dressed up as the real thing. Left as its own follow-up, sized once there is real signal (a dismissal, a complaint, an eval subset) that lexical match is actually missing facts in practice — inventing that signal now would be exactly the kind of premature abstraction `AGENTS.md` §4 argues against elsewhere.

Keeping `retrieved_facts`/`retrieved_notes` separate from `memory_fact` rather than merging them (e.g., appending the resolved fact into `retrieved_facts` and dropping the singular parameter) was the other real choice here. `memory_fact` is a single fact already known, at composition time, to settle one specific detected conflict — the model is told to use it and say "Resolved by memory: ...". `retrieved_facts`/`retrieved_notes` are unranked background that may or may not bear on anything the answer ends up saying, always passed once retrieval clears the abstention threshold, whether or not a clarification was just answered. Folding the two together would have meant either always treating retrieved background as conflict-resolving (wrong — most of it is not) or teaching the prompt to distinguish "the one entry that resolves things" from "the rest" inside a single block, which is more complex than two blocks with two different jobs.

**Consequences:** A future embedding-based upgrade (issue #292) changes `retrieve_relevant_facts`'s internals only — its signature and `RelevantMemory` return shape do not need to change, so `askwell.ask` and `askwell.agent.conflict` are unaffected whenever that lands. Citing a memory fact or schema note the way a document citation resolves to a chunk is explicitly not built here (`M3-APPLY-BE-079`'s own scope) — the new prompt section tells the model plainly that citing memory this way is not yet supported, so it does not invent a numbered citation for one. Closes issue #293 (no test previously exercised `_run_generation`'s wiring of retrieval into `messages.trace`/`audit_interactions` — three new cases in `test_ask_api.py` do now); issue #292 stays open, re-owned as above rather than closed, since the assumption it names is still not true.

**Refs:** `docs/backlog/M3-it-learns-my-material.md` `M3-APPLY-RET-078`; `docs/memory-and-clarification.md` §3, §6; issues #292, #293; `api/src/askwell/memory.py` (`retrieve_relevant_facts`, `RelevantMemory`); `api/src/askwell/agent/conflict.py`; `api/src/askwell/agent/prompts/conflicting_sources.v1.md`; `api/src/askwell/ask.py` (`_run_generation`).

## 2026-09-17 — `M3-REVIEW-FE-075`: the capped disclosure is read independently of the pending queue, and the completion state is triggered from the event handler that empties it, not from an effect watching derived state

**Decision:** Two things this ticket had to actually decide, beyond wiring up five states.

First, the capped state (issue #297, filed when `-074` shipped the capped-only-alongside-pending case and explicitly deferred the capped-only-with-nothing-pending case): `askwell.review._capped_counts` reads `audit_decisions` for `clarification_capped` records directly and independently of `list_pending`'s own pending-only query, then folds the result into `list_pending`'s groups — attaching a `capped` count to an existing group where one already exists from the pending query, and synthesising a zero-item group where it does not. The alternative from #297's own options list — leaving this to a future "review inferred facts" surface in Memory — was rejected because the copy this ticket ships (`"you can review them in Memory"`) is a promise the clarifications screen itself makes; a screen that cannot keep its own promise once its queue empties is a worse failure than the modest complexity of a second, independent query.

Second, the completion state: `ClarificationsScreen.removeItem`/`removeGroup` call `maybeShowCompletion(total)` directly, as plain event-handler code, rather than a `useEffect` watching a derived `hasGroups` boolean transition to `false`. The first version of this (a `useEffect([hasGroups])` that called `setState` synchronously in its body) tripped `react-hooks/set-state-in-effect` in `scripts/dev.sh web-run pnpm exec eslint` — React's own guidance is that synchronising state *derived from other React state* inside an effect is the anti-pattern the hook flags, not a lint config to silence. The event-handler version also happens to be more correct than the effect it replaced: it can tell "the group list just became empty because of the action that is currently resolving" apart from "the group list was already empty," which a `hasGroups`-transition effect cannot — the effect fires once per false→true→false cycle, but has no way to distinguish "always was empty, no new tally entry" from "just now emptied." A `stateRef`, synced by a genuinely side-effect-free `useEffect` (it only assigns a ref, calls no setter — lint has nothing to say about it), is what lets `removeItem`/`removeGroup` read up-to-date group state without adding `state` itself to their own `useCallback` dependency list, which would rebuild the 10-second undo timer on every unrelated fetch.

**Why:** Both choices trace back to the same principle the ticket's own Acceptance Criteria state directly — "the screen never blocks and never nags" — read as "the screen never tells the user something that is not currently true." A capped-only group with no items and no "Skip all" button, a completion sentence that says "5 answered" only when five things were actually answered rather than merely no-longer-pending (an item removed by undo, or by a group dismiss, is not an answer, and `completionSentence` treats a dismiss as a skip rather than silently rounding it into the answered count), and a re-processing card whose failure names the actual `error` string a worker wrote rather than a hardcoded "stopped, not stuck" (issue #299) are the same discipline applied three times. The `kind` field added to `Reprocessing` (issue #300) is the same idea at the type level: `askwell.clarify` cannot yet raise a table-shaped clarification (M4's own scope, `column_distribution` evidence exists as a shape with no producer), so shipping "table" as a possible value now, always empty in practice, was weighed against shipping "document" only and adding `kind` in a second migration once M4 lands. The second option was rejected: `_reprocessing_summary` is exactly the function that already knows which evidence shape it is looking at (it is what makes the per-item `savedConfirmation` label correct today), so the cost of naming `kind` now is one ternary, and the cost of retrofitting it later is finding every caller of `Reprocessing` that assumed a fixed noun.

**Consequences:** `_capped_counts` runs one query per `GET /clarifications` regardless of whether any source is actually capped — negligible at one-user-one-machine scale, and the alternative (a flag on `sources` set at raise time, checked before deciding whether to run the query) was rejected as a second source of truth for a fact `audit_decisions` already answers, which is exactly the kind of drift the audit stores exist to prevent. Undoing an answer does not currently roll back that answer's contribution to the session tally (stated as a known gap in `CHANGELOG.md` — the tally is scratch, not a persisted record, and undo is rare enough that a slightly-inflated completion sentence after an undo was judged acceptable rather than worth threading `handleUndo` through the tally as well). The "N tables" half of `completionSentence` is exercised by unit tests constructing `Reprocessing(kind="table", ...)` directly, not by any real trigger — accurate to say plainly rather than claim a live walkthrough that cannot exist until M4.

**Refs:** `docs/backlog/M3-it-learns-my-material.md` `M3-REVIEW-FE-075`; `docs/ux/clarifications.md` §5; issues #297, #299, #300; `api/src/askwell/review.py` `_capped_counts`, `_reprocessing_summary`; `web/components/clarifications/clarifications-screen.tsx`, `reapply-progress.tsx`; `web/lib/clarifications.ts`.

## 2026-09-17 — `M3-INLINE-FE-085`: only a contradiction or document-identity clarification can block, matched exact-subject-or-shared-document, and the paused turn resumes through the existing `memory_fact` hook rather than a new resolution path

**Decision:** `askwell.inline_clarify.find_blocking` only ever interrupts on the two triggers `clarify.py` ranks highest, `contradiction` and `document_identity` — never an abbreviation or a poor scan, both of which can be skipped without changing which fact the answer states. Relevance is exact: the clarification's `subject` appears as a substring of the question, or one of the documents its evidence names was actually retrieved for this question; no embedding or fuzzy match. A turn with more than one relevant ambiguity asks about the highest-ranked one and defers the rest to the ordinary queue, naming the count in the answer, rather than asking in sequence. Once resolved, the answer is composed by passing the resolved fact into `compose_conflict`'s existing `memory_fact` parameter — built by `M2-PARTIAL-BE-059` and left inert ever since — rather than inventing a second way to inject a fact into the prompt. A skip does not fall back to presenting both sides (compose_conflict's ordinary un-resolved behaviour); it computes a default assumption in Python (the newer-dated passage for a contradiction, the newest document for a document-identity ambiguity) and states it as an appended line, since the acceptance criterion is that the answer *names* what it assumed, not that it hedges.

**Why:** Blocking on every trigger kind would make this the "modal per question" pattern `docs/memory-and-clarification.md` §2 already rejected for ingestion, just moved to answer time instead — an abbreviation the user does not know does not change the *fact* the answer states, only its wording, so it belongs in the queue exactly as before. Fuzzy relevance matching was rejected for the same reason `askwell.clarify._known_facts` gives for exact-subject suppression: a near-miss that interrupts a question the ambiguity has nothing to do with is a worse failure than an occasional missed interruption, which the ordinary post-hoc queue still catches. Asking multiple ambiguities together (as a single combined inline card) was considered and rejected as materially larger scope for a ticket whose own Known Gaps line explicitly allows deferring the second — a combined card also means "answering" is no longer a single write to a single `memory` row, which is exactly the kind of parallel-path complexity `M3-CORRECT-BE-082`'s own decision (above) already argues against elsewhere in this milestone. Reusing `memory_fact` rather than building a new resolution channel means the prompt-level behaviour ("Resolved by memory: ...", never silently presenting both sides once a fact exists) was already written and eval'd as part of `M2-PARTIAL-BE-059` — wiring an existing, documented hook is a smaller and more auditable change than inventing a second one that does almost the same thing. A skip inventing a *guessed* fact and writing it to `memory` was rejected outright: `askwell.clarify` deliberately stores no `inferred_fact` for either blocking trigger because there is nothing safe to guess (a wrong guess is fed straight back into future prompts) — a skip therefore states its assumption only in the one answer it affects, and asks again on any later turn that actually needs it, rather than polluting memory with an unconfirmed value.

**Consequences:** A blocking clarification's evidence must now carry `evidence.trigger` (added by this ticket to `askwell.clarify.raise_candidates`) for `find_blocking` to identify it — a row raised by a version of `clarify.py` predating this ticket has no `trigger` key and will never be treated as blocking, which is acceptable since M3 has not shipped and there is no such row anywhere but a test fixture. A turn paused on a clarification holds an `asyncio.Event` on the in-memory `_Turn` object; a process restart while a turn is paused loses that turn exactly the way any other in-flight turn is lost today (`reconcile_interrupted` marks it `failed`), which is unchanged from before this ticket and not a new risk it introduces. Combining two ambiguities into one inline card is deferred as its own follow-up rather than built now.

**Refs:** `docs/backlog/M3-it-learns-my-material.md` `M3-INLINE-FE-085`; `docs/ux/ask.md` §5; `docs/ux/clarifications.md` §5; `api/src/askwell/inline_clarify.py`; `api/src/askwell/ask.py`; `api/src/askwell/agent/conflict.py`.

## 2026-09-17 — `M3-CORRECT-BE-082`: the correction path is the existing store functions extended in place, not a new parallel layer

**Decision:** `M3-CORRECT-BE-082`'s "one correction path used by both the chip and the memory screen" is built by extending `askwell.memory.correct_memory_fact`/`correct_schema_note`/`delete_memory_fact`/`delete_schema_note` themselves — adding a row lock, a same-value no-op check, and re-processing via `askwell.reapply` directly inside each — rather than adding new wrapper functions (`correct_and_reprocess_*`) on top of the untouched `M3-STORE-BE-076` primitives. Their return types changed from a bare `uuid.UUID`/`None` to `CorrectionOutcome`/`DeletionOutcome`, and `-076`'s own tests in `api/tests/test_memory.py` were updated to match rather than left targeting a superseded contract.

**Why:** No route or UI calls these functions yet — both dependent callers (`M3-CORRECT-FE-081`, the chip; `M3-MEM-FE-083`/`-084`, the memory screen) are still open tickets — so there is no existing consumer whose contract a signature change could break, which is precisely what made extending in place safe here and would not be safe once a route exists. The alternative, a second set of `*_and_reprocess` functions, was rejected as the exact anti-pattern the ticket's own Business Value warns about: "two correction paths that behave differently is how a fact ends up half-corrected" applies just as much to two *functions* behind one screen as to two screens, and future code reaching for the shorter old name after both exist is a realistic mistake. `resolve_dependencies`/`enqueue` in `askwell.reapply` also had a latent bug surfaced by making `clarification_id` optional: the conflict query's `id != :id` compares to SQL `NULL` and is never true, so a `NULL` `clarification_id` silently excluded every pending clarification from the conflict search rather than considering all of them — invisible until a caller without a clarification (this ticket) existed to exercise it. Fixed with `(CAST(:id AS uuid) IS NULL OR id != :id)` rather than working around it by inventing a sentinel UUID, which would have been the kind of guess `AGENTS.md` §4 rules out. A `schema_note`-kind dependency for a schema-note correction is dropped rather than promoted, because `reapply.run_job` promotes it using the `memory` row's `fact` text and a schema-note correction has no such row — promoting with an empty string was rejected outright as silent corruption of a different, unrelated schema note.

**Consequences:** Any future code that already called `correct_memory_fact` for its old `uuid.UUID` return value would need updating — none did, but the next reader touching this file should know the signature changed under this ticket, not `-076`. The `schema_note`-kind filtering means a schema-note correction can leave a genuinely stale inferred note on a different table unpromoted when it happens to share the corrected column's name; this is the same "errs toward re-processing more, matches less precisely" trade-off `M3-APPLY-ING-080` already accepted, applied to a case that ticket did not anticipate.

**Refs:** `docs/backlog/M3-it-learns-my-material.md` `M3-CORRECT-BE-082`; `api/src/askwell/memory.py`; `api/src/askwell/reapply.py`; `api/tests/test_memory.py`.

## 2026-09-17 — `M3-APPLY-ING-080`: re-processing re-embeds every chunk of an affected document rather than a text-matched subset, and an undo does not revert already-finished items

**Decision:** Dependency resolution treats every chunk of every document `askwell.review._reprocessing_summary`'s own evidence-derived set already names (or every live document in the source, when evidence names none) as affected — not a subset filtered by whether the chunk's text contains the clarification's subject literally. A `schema_note`/`conflict` dependency is matched by exact subject-to-table/column-name equality, case-insensitive, never fuzzy. Separately: `undo_answer` cancels only the *not-yet-run* items of a job tied to the undone clarification; an item already `done` (a chunk already re-embedded, a schema note already promoted to the user's answer) is left as it is rather than reverted.

**Why:** The ticket's own Assumption states dependency resolution "is approximate and errs toward re-processing more rather than less" — a chunk can be relevant to a subject without containing its literal spelling (an abbreviation used once, then only referred to by what it stands for), so text-matching to shrink the set was rejected as the wrong direction to err in for a feature whose entire value proposition is that answering visibly changes something. Reusing the exact same evidence-derived document set the confirmation toast already names (rather than a second, independent resolution) was the alternative to inventing a new "what does this subject touch" query — and the ticket names avoiding a second source of truth for that question as a real risk (`docs/ux/clarifications.md` §4's toast and the queue's actual work must agree). For undo: reverting a completed schema-note promotion means writing a fresh `inferred` note back — indistinguishable in shape from what `askwell.clarify` would infer on a later pass — while reverting a re-embedded chunk means nothing at all, since nothing about a chunk's *content* changed; the only asymmetry an undo can meaningfully fix is stopping *future* work from applying a fact that is now gone, which is what cancellation does. Building full reversal of already-applied work was rejected as disproportionate to the ticket's own upper-bound granularity note, and is filed as a known gap rather than silently absent.

**Consequences:** A large source can queue substantially more re-embed work per answer than a text-matched subset would, which is a real cost (the ticket's own edge case: "thousands of chunks — batched, with progress, and the machine stays usable" is what the per-item queue and inline retry exist to absorb, not avoid). Undoing an answer after some of its re-processing has already finished leaves that finished work in place — a person expecting an undo to be a full rollback will be surprised, and this is the first thing to revisit if that surprises a real user rather than only a reviewer.

**Refs:** `docs/backlog/M3-it-learns-my-material.md` `M3-APPLY-ING-080`; `docs/memory-and-clarification.md` §1, §2; `api/src/askwell/reapply.py`; `api/src/askwell/review.py` `documents_named_in_evidence`, `undo_answer`; issue #264.

## 2026-09-17 — `M3-REVIEW-FE-074`: the confirmation's document count is honest-inflated, never a fabricated specific

**Decision:** `answer_clarification` names the documents an abbreviation's passage, a contradiction's passages, or `document_identity`'s own `options` already point at, when evidence carries them. When it does not — an abbreviation whose evidence sampling (`EVIDENCE_MAX_SAMPLES = 2`) missed most of the documents it occurs in, or a `poor_scan`/`unavailable` shape that names none — the confirmation falls back to a fresh count of every live document in the source, worded "N documents in this source" rather than a specific list.

**Why:** The ticket's Validation Rule is that the confirmation names the affected material *specifically*, but the evidence a `-071` candidate stored is deliberately bounded — it was captured to make the question answerable, not to be a complete index of every document the subject touches. Re-deriving a precise list at answer time (grepping every document's content for the subject) was rejected: it is the kind of query `docs/memory-and-clarification.md`'s own trigger-detection Assumption says must stay cheap, and doing it synchronously inside the answer transaction risks exactly the "computable quickly enough" Assumption this ticket names. A source-wide document count is always true, computed with one indexed count query, and never invents a document name nobody's evidence backed — the alternative, silently saying "some material" with no number, is the generic toast this ticket exists to replace.

**Consequences:** The count can overstate what genuinely depends on an abbreviation answer (every document in the source, not just the ones using it) until `M3-APPLY-ING-080` does real dependency resolution and can report exactly what it touched. Fine for now — re-reading is a no-op regardless until that ticket lands, and the Known Gap this ticket names is explicit about the count describing what *would* be re-read, not a promise already kept.

**Refs:** `docs/backlog/M3-it-learns-my-material.md` `M3-REVIEW-FE-074`; `api/src/askwell/review.py` `_reprocessing_summary`; `docs/ux/clarifications.md` §4.

## 2026-09-17 — `M3-STORE-OBS-077`: undo takes the fact's id from the caller, not by inference from the clarification row

**Decision:** `askwell.review.undo_answer(session, clarification_id, memory_id)` requires the caller to pass back the `memory_id` that `answer_clarification` returned, rather than looking it up from `clarifications.subject` + `clarifications.answer` at undo time. It also refuses (`CannotUndo`) if that memory row is no longer the active one for its subject.

**Why:** matching by `subject`/`answer` text is exactly the kind of implicit link the audit chain exists to replace with something explicit — two clarifications could share a subject, or the fact text could be edited between answer and undo, and either would make a text match either miss or hit the wrong row. The ticket's own edge case ("undo of a save is recorded as its own decision, not by removing the original record") already implies the two events are linked by identity, not by re-derivation; `answer_clarification` already hands the id back in `AnswerOutcome`, so the frontend's undo window (`M3-REVIEW-FE-074`) has it for free and does not need a second lookup. Refusing when the fact has since been superseded was the harder call: the alternative (deleting whatever the *current* active fact for the subject is) would silently discard a correction the user made on purpose, in the name of undoing something older and less relevant than what replaced it.

**Consequences:** `M3-REVIEW-FE-074`'s undo control must carry the `memory_id` from the answer response through its undo window rather than only the `clarification_id`. If a correction lands inside that window, undo surfaces as refused rather than silently doing nothing or doing the wrong thing — `docs/states-and-edge-cases.md` should get that state when `-074` builds the UI for it.

**Refs:** `docs/backlog/M3-it-learns-my-material.md` `M3-STORE-OBS-077`, `api/src/askwell/review.py` (`undo_answer`, `CannotUndo`), `api/tests/test_review.py` (`test_undo_after_the_answer_was_corrected_is_refused`).

## 2026-09-17 — `M3-STORE-BE-076`'s own acceptance criteria closed (#283, #284), not deferred as caller discipline

**Decision:** `write_memory_fact`/`write_schema_note` (`api/src/askwell/memory.py`) now (1) default `confidence` to `FULL_CONFIDENCE` for any user-origin write left unset, and (2) supersede *any* existing active fact/note for the subject/position on a user-origin write, not only an inferred one — folding what was previously only `correct_memory_fact`/`correct_schema_note`'s behaviour into the plain write path too. Chose issue #283's and #284's own "Recommended" option (make the module safe by construction) over the alternative each issue also offered (require every caller to route a second user answer through `correct_*`, or enforce it with a database-level partial unique index).

**Why:** both gaps directly contradicted this ticket's own stated acceptance criteria — "Answering a clarification writes a fact with origin clarification and full confidence" (#283) and the "two contradicting user answers" edge case (#284) — not a separate concern found later. `#283`'s existing test (`test_answering_a_clarification_writes_a_full_confidence_user_fact`) asserted `confidence is None` with a comment excusing the gap, which is exactly the "invariant asserted in a docstring but not enforced in code" pattern `AGENTS.md` §4 already warns against. Rejected the database-unique-index option (#284's option 2) because it pushes conflict handling onto every future caller instead of the module itself, and rejected leaving it to caller discipline (#284's option 3, #283's option 2) for the same reason both issues did: `#282`'s rewiring of `clarify.py`/`review.py` is exactly the kind of new caller that would otherwise inherit the bug silently. Applied the same fix to `write_schema_note` for symmetry, even though #284 named only `memory` — the ticket's own granularity note calls this "two stores sharing one supersession rule," and leaving schema notes with the weaker guarantee would have been an inconsistency nobody asked for.

**Consequences:** A second `write_memory_fact`/`write_schema_note` call with a user-origin for a subject/position that already has an active user-origin row now supersedes it, matching `correct_memory_fact`/`correct_schema_note`'s behaviour, so callers no longer need to know which of the two functions to use to avoid a double-active row. `#282` (rewiring `clarify.py`/`review.py` onto this module) is unaffected in scope — still its own ticket — but now inherits the corrected default rather than the gap. `#262`/`#263`/`#264` (dependents) needed no change; none of them call `write_memory_fact`/`write_schema_note` with an explicit `confidence` that this default would now override incorrectly.

**Refs:** issues #283, #284, #282 (re-owned, unchanged); `api/src/askwell/memory.py`; `api/tests/test_memory.py`; `CHANGELOG.md` `0.3.9`.

## 2026-09-17 — `M3-STORE-BE-076` rebuilt fresh against current `main`, not rebased from the parked attempt

**Decision:** Reimplemented `M3-STORE-BE-076` (`api/src/askwell/memory.py`, `api/tests/test_memory.py`, migration `2ae457a0587a`) by porting the parked branch's (`origin/parked/m3-store-be-076-attempt-1`) `memory.py`, `test_memory.py` and `memory.source_id` migration verbatim onto current `main`, rather than rebasing the whole branch as issue #276 recommended.

**Why:** the parked branch's own diff against `main` touches 39 files, most of it churn from having been branched before `M3-RAISE-BE-071`, `M3-REVIEW-BE-072a` and `M3-REVIEW-FE-072`/`073` — it deletes `review.py`, `web/app/clarifications/`, and most of `clarify.py`'s current shape, none of which this ticket's own scope (a write-side module for `memory`/`schema_notes`) touches. A full rebase would have required re-deriving three tickets' worth of conflict resolution to land one ticket's worth of new code — the module itself (`memory.py`) was untouched by any of the three tickets since it never merged, so it applies cleanly standalone. `docs/db/models.py`'s `MemoryFact`/`SchemaNote` columns (`origin`, `confidence`, `superseded_by`) already existed on `main` (`M3-RAISE-BE-070`'s 2026-08-30 finding, reconfirmed here); only `memory.source_id` needed a new migration, chained onto the current head (`a4d9e2f6c831`) rather than the parked branch's now-stale one. Issue #258's gap (no test reads `superseded_by` back directly on a retired inferred schema note) is fixed in the same change, folded into `test_writing_and_correcting_a_schema_note` as its own recommendation asked, rather than left for a follow-up ticket.

**Consequences:** `write_memory_fact`/`write_schema_note`/`correct_memory_fact`/`correct_schema_note`/`get_active_memory_facts`/`get_active_schema_notes` exist as the dedicated write-side surface the ticket describes, but nothing in `clarify.py` or `review.py` was rewired to call them — both still use their own inline `INSERT`s (`M3-RAISE-BE-068`/`070`/`071`, `M3-REVIEW-BE-072a`), which is unchanged from before this ticket and was already the state the 2026-09-13/-17 decisions log entries for those tickets reasoned about. Whether those call sites should move onto this module is a separate, smaller change than reintroducing it — filed as follow-up rather than done speculatively here.

**Refs:** `docs/backlog/M3-it-learns-my-material.md` `M3-STORE-BE-076`; issues #276, #258, #262, #263, #264; `api/src/askwell/memory.py`; `api/src/askwell/db/migrations/versions/20260917_2ae457a0587a_memory_source_id.py`.

## 2026-09-17 — `M3-REVIEW-FE-073` renders evidence without a source link, since evidence has no document id

**Decision:** Item anatomy (`clarifications-screen.tsx`) renders a passage's document name and page as plain mono text, with no click-through to the source viewer, rather than inventing a link or blocking the ticket on the gap.

**Why:** `docs/ux/clarifications.md` §3's own edge case calls for a long passage to be "truncated with a link to the source." `documentHref` (`web/lib/citations.ts`) needs a `documentId`; every evidence shape `askwell.clarify` writes (`passage`, `poor_scan`, `contradiction`, `document_identity`) carries only a `filename`/`document` string, confirmed by reading `clarify.py`'s four detectors directly and by seeding real rows through `GET /clarifications`. Adding `document_id` to evidence is a `clarify.py` change — out of scope for a ticket whose own Scope line is "item rendering," and speculatively touching it here would be exactly the un-agreed multi-file change §4 says to stop and propose first rather than take. Filed as #280 (its own issue, not folded into #251, since it's not poor-scan-specific) instead of silently shipping a broken-looking link or stubbing one.

**Consequences:** The AC's "truncated ... with a link" is only half met — truncation exists (server-side, `_bound_text`, unchanged by this ticket), the link does not, until #280 lands. Reversing this means either accepting evidence as filename-only permanently, or doing #280's backend change first.

**Refs:** `docs/ux/clarifications.md` §3, `web/lib/citations.ts::documentHref`, `api/src/askwell/clarify.py`, issues #280, #251.

## 2026-09-17 — `M3-RAISE-BE-071`'s evidence shape reuses the parked prior attempt, and drops the old key names

**Decision:** Rebuilt `M3-RAISE-BE-071` from scratch against current `main` (`8870b0f9`) rather than resurrecting the local unmerged commit on `feat/m3-raise-be-071` (#275) or the closed PR's branch, but reused the evidence shape from `origin/parked/m3-raise-be-071-attempt-1` almost verbatim — it diffs cleanly against `M3-RAISE-BE-068`'s own commit and its reasoning (`kind`-tagged evidence, `_bound_text` at 500 chars, `EVIDENCE_MAX_SAMPLES`/`EVIDENCE_MAX_COLUMN_VALUES` bounds, an `unavailable` kind for the edge case) was already sound. This changes `clarifications.evidence`'s per-trigger keys: `{"occurrences": n}` becomes `{"kind": "passage", "occurrences": n, "samples": [...]}`, `{"filenames": [...]}` (document identity) becomes `{"kind": "passage", "samples": [...]}`, `{"low_confidence_pages": [...], "total_pages": n}` becomes `{"kind": "poor_scan", "pages": [...], "total_pages": n, "extracted_text": [...], "page_images": "not available"}`, and `{"values": [...]}` (contradiction) becomes `{"kind": "contradiction", "passages": [...]}` with each passage carrying its own page, date and bounded excerpt.

**Why:** `docs/decisions.md`'s 2026-09-17 entry above already established that `072a`'s consumer only passes `evidence` through opaquely, so this rename has no caller to break on `main` today — confirmed by reading `askwell.review.list_pending`, which does `"evidence": evidence` with no key access. `_rank_candidates`' own weighting (`_rank_weight`) did read two of the renamed keys (`filenames`, `values`) directly; both were repointed at `candidate.options`/`evidence["passages"]` respectively rather than left silently broken, since `M3-RAISE-BE-069`/`070`'s ranking behaviour must not regress as a side effect of this ticket. The page-image gap for poor scans is stated honestly (`"page_images": "not available"`) rather than invented or silently dropped, per #251 which this ticket's own AC already tracks separately as pipeline scope, not clarify.py scope.

**Consequences:** Any future reader of `clarifications.evidence` must match on `evidence["kind"]` rather than assuming a fixed key set — documented in `clarify.py`'s own module docstring. `M3-REVIEW-FE-073`, which renders this shape, was blocked (#275) specifically because this shape was not reachable from `main`; it can now proceed. Reversing this means going back to flat, untagged evidence dicts with no `current_inference` prefill and no truncation bound — not recommended, since both were named acceptance criteria.

**Refs:** `api/src/askwell/clarify.py`, `api/tests/test_clarify.py`, issues #275, #256, #251, `origin/parked/m3-raise-be-071-attempt-1`.

## 2026-09-17 — `M3-REVIEW-BE-072a` built despite its two named dependencies not being separately closed

**Decision:** Built `GET /clarifications`, `POST /clarifications/{id}/answer` and `POST /clarifications/{id}/skip` (`api/src/askwell/review.py`) even though the ticket's own Dependencies — `M3-RAISE-BE-071` (evidence capture) and `M3-STORE-BE-076` (a dedicated memory write-side module) — are neither one built as their own ticket.

**Why:** both dependencies exist here only as *capability*, not as a specific ticket closing, and the capability is what this ticket actually needs. `clarifications.evidence` already carries real data — occurrence counts, filenames, low-confidence pages — written by `askwell.clarify` under `M3-RAISE-BE-068`/`069`; `-071`'s own scope (richer per-trigger evidence: full passages, bounded truncation, an inference prefill) is a superset improvement to that column's *content*, not a precondition for reading whatever is in it today, and `072a`'s own scope is only "expose the payload," not "improve its capture." `M3-STORE-BE-076`'s columns (`memory.origin`, `.confidence`, `.superseded_by`) already exist from the initial schema migration — confirmed the same way `docs/decisions.md`'s 2026-08-30 entry for `M3-RAISE-BE-070` confirmed it for that ticket's own lookup — so writing a `memory` row with `origin = 'clarification'` needs no abstraction `076` hasn't built yet; it needs the same raw `INSERT` `askwell.clarify` already uses in three other places. The rejected alternative was stopping and filing a third dependency-blocked issue, which would have repeated `070`'s own finding rather than adding anything a future reader does not already have.

**Consequences:** `076`'s own scope — a shared module for origin/confidence/supersession mechanics, rather than each caller hand-writing the same `INSERT` — remains genuinely open, and `api/src/askwell/review.py` is now a second hand-written copy of that pattern alongside `askwell.clarify`'s. Whichever ticket finally builds `076` should fold both call sites into it rather than leaving `review.py`'s copy to drift. Issues [#257](https://github.com/Rumeasiyan/askwell/issues/257) and [#268](https://github.com/Rumeasiyan/askwell/issues/268) (no `GET /clarifications` endpoint, filed twice by build agents that stopped rather than invent the contract) are closed by this ticket.

**Refs:** `api/src/askwell/review.py`, `api/tests/test_review.py`, `api/tests/test_review_api.py`, `docs/backlog/M3-it-learns-my-material.md` (`M3-REVIEW-BE-072a`), issues #257, #268.

## 2026-09-13 — Memory-suppression is one check across all four triggers, not per-trigger dedup

**Decision:** `M3-RAISE-BE-070` checks every candidate's subject against `memory` and `schema_notes` in one place, after all four triggers have run and before the pass/fail tests, rather than teaching each trigger its own dedup rule. The abbreviation trigger's existing inline memory pre-filter (`M3-RAISE-BE-068`) was removed in favour of this — it did the same lookup but silently, with no decisions-store record.

**Why:** the ticket's own scope is "one lookup and one suppression rule," and the four triggers already disagree enough on shape (abbreviation text, filenames, normalised stems, extracted phrases) that a per-trigger version would be four slightly-different lookups nobody could audit as a single guarantee. Centralising also fixed a real gap: the abbreviation trigger's pre-filter never logged what it suppressed, so "check memory before raising" held for abbreviations but was unverifiable — no record existed to prove it. The rejected alternative was leaving abbreviations' silent skip in place and adding logged suppression only to the other three triggers; that would have left one trigger's behaviour unlike the rest for no reason a future reader could find in the code.

`schema_notes` is checked as a fallback, matched against `table_name`/`column_name`, even though none of the four M3 triggers currently produce a subject shaped like one — the ticket names it as an explicit touchpoint (`memory-and-clarification.md`'s "memory and schema notes are checked") and the M4 column-ambiguity trigger will produce exactly that shape of subject when it lands, at which point this path stops being unused rather than needing to be added.

**Consequences:** every suppression, including the abbreviation case, now writes a `clarification_suppressed` decisions record naming the fact applied, and counts in `RaiseResult.suppressed`. A subject match is exact only — `docs/backlog/M3-it-learns-my-material.md`'s own Assumption for this ticket rules out fuzzy matching, since a false suppression (asking never happens, and the user never finds out it should have) is worse than the duplicate question it would have prevented.

**Refs:** `api/src/askwell/clarify.py` (`_known_facts`, `raise_candidates`), `api/tests/test_clarify.py`, `docs/backlog/M3-it-learns-my-material.md` (`M3-RAISE-BE-070`).

## 2026-08-30 — `document_identity` ranks second, ahead of abbreviations, though `docs/memory-and-clarification.md` §8 never names it

**Decision:** `M3-RAISE-BE-069`. `askwell.clarify._rank_candidates` orders passing candidates: `contradiction` (0), `document_identity` (1), `abbreviation` (2), `unreadable_scan` (3), each tier then sorted by a per-trigger weight (occurrence count, page count, or document count) descending, subject ascending as the final deterministic tie-break. The cap (`get_clarification_cap`/`set_clarification_cap`, `settings` table, default 5) is applied to that ranked list; anything below it is written to `memory` as a low-confidence inference naming its own rank and the cap, not the candidate's own guessed answer — that stays reserved for a trigger whose `inferred_fact` is genuinely a defensible guess, which contradiction and document identity never have.

**Why:** the ranking rule in `docs/memory-and-clarification.md` §8 is "contradictions, then date-format ambiguity, then unguessable columns, then abbreviations, then low-confidence scans" — five named tiers, and `document_identity` (`M3-RAISE-BE-068`'s own trigger, "three files look like versions of the same document") is in none of them. Two of the five named tiers (date-format, unguessable columns) don't exist yet either — they arrive with M4's data sources — so the real choice was where to slot one trigger the document never anticipated. Treating it as lowest priority was rejected: a document-identity question is not a vocabulary gap like an abbreviation, it is "which file's facts are current", the same shape of consequence as a contradiction — get it wrong and the wrong document's numbers get treated as authoritative, silently. Ranking it above abbreviations but below actual contradictions (a contradiction is a confirmed factual disagreement; document identity is a suspected one) was the closest fit to the document's own stated reasoning without inventing a sixth tier the PRD process never signed off on. This is a default, not a product decision — `AGENTS.md`'s "decide defaults, don't hand back" applies; a `docs/PRD.md` §11 item this is not.

**Consequences:** `docs/memory-and-clarification.md` §8 should eventually name `document_identity` explicitly rather than leaving the next reader to rediscover this gap; not corrected in that file this round because the ticket's own scope was the ranking function and cap, not an audit of every trigger the ranking table should list. A capped-out contradiction or document-identity candidate never gets a guessed answer written to memory — its inferred fact is deliberately generic ("not asked — ranked N of M, below the cap") — because inventing which document is current, or which side of a contradiction is right, is exactly the fabrication C5 exists to prevent; only the fact that it was flagged and not reached is safe to record. The edge case in the ticket's own Acceptance Criteria — a late-arriving high-ranking candidate displacing an unanswered lower-ranked one from an earlier ingestion batch — is not built: `raise_candidates` still scans a source exactly once, guarded by "does it already have any clarification row", so a source added to incrementally locks in whatever ranked in the top 5 at first scan. Filed as issue #249 rather than built here; real design work (what happens to a pending clarification's `evidence` when the underlying data changes between scans) that risks scope creep beyond this ticket.

**Refs:** `docs/memory-and-clarification.md` §8; `docs/ux/clarifications.md` §5, §8; `AGENTS.md` "decide defaults, don't hand back"; ticket `M3-RAISE-BE-069`; issue #249; `api/src/askwell/clarify.py`.

## 2026-08-29 — The eval gate runs on the maintainer's own machine as a self-hosted runner, not a hosted one; the first attempt's rejection was a stale diff, not a workflow defect

**Decision:** `M2-EVAL-DEPLOY-067`'s `.github/workflows/eval.yml` runs `runs-on: [self-hosted, askwell]` rather than `ubuntu-latest`. It triggers on a push or PR touching a prompt file, `retrieve.py`, `config.py`, a suite file, or a suite-mode module, plus `workflow_dispatch`, brings up the maintainer's *own persistent* Compose stack (reusing `.env` and volumes rather than fabricating throwaway CI credentials the way `ci.yml` does), self-heals only the generation model weight from `api/src/askwell/models_catalog.py`'s registry-verified spec, and fails the job with a named reason — rather than guessing at a download — if the embedding or reranker weight is missing, since neither has an automated, registry-verified fetch source yet (`AGENTS.md` §4 forbids inventing one). The prior attempt (`origin/parked/m2-eval-deploy-067-attempt-1`, `a1f178e4`, "wip: M2-EVAL-DEPLOY-067, not accepted") wrote essentially this same workflow — same triggers, same self-heal/fail-clear split, same infra-vs-score distinction on exit codes — but was branched before `M2-PARTIAL-FE-058` landed, so its diff against `main` also *reverted* that ticket's `documents.py`/`ask-screen.tsx`/`answer-annotations.ts`/`document-dates.ts` work and deleted `M2-PARTIAL-FE-058`'s own manual-test doc. Parked rather than merged for that reason alone, not because the workflow itself was wrong. This pass rewrites the workflow fresh against current `main` rather than cherry-picking the stale diff, carrying the design forward unchanged except for one correction: the original cited "issue #241" for the missing embedding/reranker fetch path, but #241 had since become an unrelated BRAIN.md-staleness issue (numbers are assigned once, and #241 was filed for something else between the two attempts) — refiled as issue #244 with the same content.

**Why:** a hosted runner was rejected outright rather than attempted with a download-everything fallback: `docs/build-plan.md`'s 165-task, 8-category gate needs three loaded llama.cpp processes (generation, embedding, reranker) and real CPU/GPU minutes per run, and downloading all three on every job would need either committing unverified embedding/reranker URLs (forbidden by `AGENTS.md` §4) or building the registry-verification work `models_catalog.py` only does for the generation model today — real scope, deferred to issue #244 rather than rushed to unblock this ticket. `AGENTS.md` §1's "one user, one machine" makes the self-hosted choice not just acceptable but the *natural* one: there is exactly one machine, it already has all three weights on disk from ordinary development use, and it is the maintainer's own, so reusing its already-provisioned `.env`/Compose stack is honest rather than a security compromise a shared runner would represent. The infra-vs-score exit-code distinction was tightened from the parked attempt's `grep -q "model unavailable"` (which only catches one of several `HarnessError` messages `eval/grounded.py`/`eval/conflict.py` can raise — a broken fixture corpus says something else entirely and would have been misclassified as "scored below bar") to checking for `eval/bench.py`'s own "written to eval/results" line, which is present if and only if a report was actually produced — structurally correct rather than string-matching a moving target. The actual GitHub branch-protection required-check setup is left as a manual step, stated as such in `docs/BRAIN.md`'s next-task note, rather than attempted via `gh api` in this change — a repo-settings change with no code review trail is exactly the kind of action that should be a deliberate, visible step, not folded into a ticket's diff.

**Consequences:** the gate cannot run at all until the runner is registered with the `askwell` label — a real one-time setup step, not built by this ticket, and the "runner offline" edge case (30-minute timeout, clear error) exists because of it. A prompt change is not actually *blocked* without a run until someone sets the required-status-check in `main`'s branch protection by hand; until then, the workflow measures but does not enforce. The five remaining quality-gate categories (SQL safety, tool selection, memory application, web escalation, and text-to-SQL) are not run by this workflow — it is named to be extended by adding a suite to the `for SUITE in ...` loop and the trigger paths, not by rewriting the gate, matching the ticket's own scope line.

**Refs:** `AGENTS.md` §1, §3 (C1), §4, §7; `docs/build-plan.md`'s quality gate; ticket `M2-EVAL-DEPLOY-067`; issues #236, #241, #244; `.github/workflows/eval.yml`; `origin/parked/m2-eval-deploy-067-attempt-1` (`a1f178e4`).

## 2026-08-29 — Conflicting-sources cards sort by date and supersession, computed once for the whole list rather than per card; the superseded-edge-case gap (#225) is left as built

**Decision:** `M2-PARTIAL-FE-058`'s first attempt (`wip: M2-PARTIAL-FE-058, not accepted`, parked at `origin/parked/m2-partial-fe-058-attempt-1`) was rejected by its own build-runner audit for issue #226 alone — every other check passed. That attempt's code (`api/src/askwell/documents.py`'s `added_at`, `web/lib/answer-annotations.ts`, `web/lib/document-dates.ts`, the `AnsweredContent`/`ConflictBanner`/`UncoveredBlock`/`ResolveOffer` split in `ask-screen.tsx`) is carried forward unchanged onto this branch, rebased past everything that landed since (`M2-DELETE-FE-062`'s deleted-card handling, `M2-FAIL-FE-060`'s degraded search). What is new here is the fix: `web/lib/document-dates.ts` gained `useDocumentDates` (plural — fetches every card's date up front, keyed by document id) and `sortByDateAndSupersession`, a pure comparator (superseded last; otherwise newest `added_at` first; a still-loading date sorts after dated cards but before superseded ones; ties keep citation-stream order via `Array.sort`'s stability). `ProvenanceMargin` and `InlineSourceCards` both call it, gated on `isConflict(...)`, before mapping cards to `<SourceCard>` — an ordinary answer's card order is untouched. Issue #225 (the superseded-edge-case can only be exercised on a *past*, re-viewed turn, since `askwell.retrieve` already excludes a superseded document from every live candidate query) is closed as filed: option 1, left as built, no synthetic path added to exercise an event the retrieval layer already prevents live.

**Why:** the original attempt's own per-card `useDocumentDate(card.documentId, showDate)` inside `SourceCard` cannot sort anything — a card fetching its own date has no way to see its siblings' dates, so the *rendering* order was always whatever `turn.citations` already held, which is citation-stream order, which is exactly the "model preference" ordering `docs/ux/ask.md` §5's own Validation Rule forbids. Fixing this needed the date lookup lifted to the list's parent, before the map that decides render order — a second, list-scoped fetch (`useDocumentDates`) rather than restructuring `SourceCard`'s own per-card fetch, which stays for the inline date/superseded label each card renders once order is already decided. Rejected: sorting inside `SourceCard` itself, which was the shape that produced the bug and cannot be fixed without seeing siblings; and folding the two fetches into one (list fetches, cards read from a passed-down map), which would touch every `SourceCard` call site including the non-conflict path for no behavioural gain — the ticket's own granularity note ("two states sharing the margin") argues for the smaller, targeted change. For #225: building a synthetic way to reopen a finished turn's citations against live document state to exercise "conflict + superseded" together would be speculative code for a scenario `askwell.agent.conflict`'s own module docstring already rules out live, and no other ticket claims that surface (`M1-VIEW-FE-048`'s context rail, the only thing that reopens past citations, does not touch conflict state).

**Consequences:** a conflicting-sources list now does two fetches per card while conflict cards are visible — one from `useDocumentDates` (sort) and one from each card's own `useDocumentDate` (label) — accepted as local-machine cost (C1 unaffected, no egress) rather than threading a fetched-date prop down to avoid the duplicate; revisit only if profiling ever shows it matters. The superseded-and-conflicting rendering path (`SourceCard`'s `supersededLabel`) stays untested end-to-end until something reopens a past conflict turn against a document superseded afterward — tracked, not blocking, per #225's own recommendation.

**Refs:** `AGENTS.md` §9 ticket `M2-PARTIAL-FE-058`; issues #225, #226; `web/lib/document-dates.ts`, `web/components/ask/provenance-margin.tsx`; `.build-runner/logs/M2-PARTIAL-FE-058.audit.log`; `origin/parked/m2-partial-fe-058-attempt-1`.

---

## 2026-08-29 — The conflict eval suite sets supersession by hand at seed time, and mean/worst reporting is now structurally paired

**Decision:** `M2-EVAL-TEST-066`'s suite (`eval/suites/conflicting_sources.v1.json`, `mode: "conflict"`) is executed by a new `eval/conflict.py`, following `eval/abstain.py`'s own shape — it reuses `eval.grounded`'s `seed_corpus`/`_ask_one` and adds only its own scoring. Ten tasks: eight genuine conflicts over two new fixture documents (`conflict_2025.pdf`/`conflict_2026.pdf`, nine same-topic facts each with a different value and its own date), one false-conflict-on-wording control (the two documents state the same gift-card fact in different words), and one superseded-document case. The superseded case needed a document relationship `sources.add()`'s own version-detection path does not offer a way to build directly in a fixture corpus — that path triggers on a *second add at the same path*, which this corpus has no reason to model — so `eval/conflict.py`'s `_ensure_superseded` sets `documents.superseded_by` with a direct, idempotent `UPDATE` after both `store_hours_2025.pdf`/`store_hours_2026.pdf` ingest normally, the same relationship `askwell.sources.add`'s `supersedes=` parameter would establish. `eval/results.py` gained `format_mean_worst(mean, worst)` — two required positional parameters, no default — as the only place the harness renders a mean; `format_summary`'s category line and every per-task line now route through it instead of building their own string, so there is no code path left that can print a mean without its paired worst-of-3.

**Why:** building the superseded relationship through the real `add()` path would mean adding the same file twice at the same path with a version decision, which is a real and already-tested code path (`api/tests`) but not one the fixture corpus's own "index once through the normal add flow" pattern (`eval.grounded.seed_corpus`, `M2-EVAL-TEST-064`'s decision) has anywhere to run twice from without restructuring how every other suite shares that corpus. Setting the relationship directly, once, after ingestion is the same trade-off `eval.grounded._ensure_root` already makes for the fixture root row — test-harness plumbing that establishes a precondition production code establishes a different way, not something `askwell.retrieve`'s own exclusion of superseded documents (`d.superseded_by IS NULL`) needs to know or care how it got there. Rejected: a from-scratch reimplementation of `sources.add()`'s version-decision flow inside the eval harness, which would be a second place that relationship gets created and drift from the real one; and skipping the superseded case entirely, which would leave the ticket's own named edge case ("a conflict where one document is superseded — the task scores that rather than conflict presentation") unmeasured. For the reporting rule: the alternative was a lint-style test asserting every `f"mean: ..."` string in the codebase is followed by `"worst-of-3"`, which is exactly the kind of check that passes today and says nothing about tomorrow's new call site — a function with two required parameters and one call site is the version of "cannot happen" that stays true after the code changes, not just today.

**Consequences:** any future eval category that needs a document already superseded before its tasks run has a documented, one-line pattern to reuse (`_ensure_superseded`'s shape) rather than needing to invent one. `eval/suite.py`'s `Task.expect_conflict`/`position_values` are `mode: "conflict"`-only fields, validated the same way `mode: "grounded"`'s `expected_documents`/`expected_passages` already are. `format_mean_worst` is now the one function every report-rendering call site must import rather than interpolating its own `mean`/`worst-of-3` string; a reviewer adding a ninth category's output should call it, not restate its format.

**Refs:** `AGENTS.md` §9 ticket `M2-EVAL-TEST-066`; `eval/conflict.py`, `eval/results.py`, `eval/suite.py`, `eval/fixtures/generate_corpus.py`, `eval/suites/conflicting_sources.v1.json`; issue #240 (fixture-generator determinism, found while regenerating the corpus for this ticket, not fixed here).

---

## 2026-08-29 — The grounded-QA eval suite reuses `askwell.ask`'s private turn machinery, and `scripts/dev.sh eval` now joins the stack's network

**Decision:** `M2-EVAL-TEST-064`'s suite (`eval/suites/grounded_qa.v1.json`, `mode: "grounded"`) is executed by a new `eval/grounded.py`, not `eval/runner.py`. `eval/runner.py` calls `InferenceClient.generate()` directly — one isolated completion, no retrieval, no citations — which was correct for `M2-EVAL-TEST-063`'s smoke suite but cannot grade "is the cited passage the right one," this ticket's core acceptance criterion. `eval/grounded.py` instead drives `askwell.ask`'s own private `_Turn`/`_generate` — the same internals `api/tests/test_ask_api.py` already reuses directly rather than going through HTTP — against a fixture corpus indexed through the real `add()` → `ingest.process()` path. Consequently, `scripts/dev.sh eval` now joins `askwell_internal` and passes a real `ASKWELL_DATABASE_URL`, the same way `db`/`test-db`/`psql` already do; it previously ran with `--network=none` and a placeholder URL, documented as "the harness never touches Postgres."

**Why:** a hand-seeded `chunks` row (`test_ask_api.py`'s own `_seed_chunk` pattern) would test the scoring code, not grounding — the ticket's own testing notes call for indexing "through the normal add flow rather than a shortcut." That requires the real database, which `M2-EVAL-TEST-063` correctly had no reason to touch yet (its one suite was pure-completion) but which a grounded suite cannot avoid. The alternative — adding a public, non-streaming "ask and get the answer back" function to `askwell.ask` purely so the harness had something less private to call — was rejected: it would be a second answer path (streaming `_generate` plus a new synchronous wrapper) to keep in sync with the one `POST /ask` actually uses, for a harness that already has a working precedent (`test_ask_api.py`) for driving the private one directly. Citation correctness is scored by matching each citation event's `filename`/`passage` against a task's `expected_documents`/`expected_passages` (either accepted, for a fact appearing in two fixture documents) rather than reusing `askwell.agent.citation_check.check_citations` (issue #182): that function checks a different thing — "did every claim get *some* citation," a structural coverage check over any stored session — not "was the cited passage the *correct* one," which needs a known-answer fixture this suite is the first thing in the repo to have.

**Consequences:** every `scripts/dev.sh eval` invocation now reaches Postgres over the internal Compose network regardless of which suite runs — a completion-only suite pays for a network hop it does not use, the same trade-off `db`/`test-db`/`psql` already accept, and still no route out (C1 is unaffected: internal-only, no egress). A future suite that must prove it needs *no* database (a pure-inference regression check) would need a documented carve-out rather than inheriting this by default. `eval/suite.py`'s `Suite.mode` and `Task.expected_documents`/`expected_passages` are now suite-format additions any future `mode: "grounded"` category (`M2-EVAL-TEST-064`'s siblings do not need this; abstention/conflict/etc. stay `mode: "completion"` unless they too need real citations) can reuse without a second format.

**Consequences (fixture corpus):** `eval/fixtures/generate_corpus.py` is the single source of the corpus's facts (a fictional company, "Meridian Loom" — deliberately invented so no task is answerable from a model's general knowledge, `AGENTS.md`'s validation rule); the generated `.pdf`/`.docx`/`.xlsx` bytes are committed under `eval/fixtures/corpus/` so the suite runs with no build step, and a fact change means re-running the generator and keeping `grounded_qa.v1.json`'s `expected_passages` in sync by hand — nothing enforces that sync automatically yet.

**Refs:** `AGENTS.md` §9 ticket `M2-EVAL-TEST-064`; `eval/grounded.py`, `eval/suite.py`, `eval/fixtures/generate_corpus.py`, `eval/suites/grounded_qa.v1.json`, `scripts/dev.sh` (`eval` case); issues #236, #182.

## 2026-08-28 — Eval harness aborts the whole run on model-unavailable; a per-run failure stays local to that run

**Decision:** `eval/runner.py` treats `InferenceUnavailable` (the model is not there at all) and `InferenceFailed` (a request to a present model failed — timeout, malformed output) differently. The first propagates and aborts the entire suite with a named reason, before or during any task, and no results file is written. The second is caught per run, recorded on that run as `{score: 0.0, error: "..."}`, and the task's other two runs still happen.

**Why:** the ticket's own edge cases name both, and conflating them would violate one or the other. If a per-task-only catch were used for `InferenceUnavailable`, a supervisor crash mid-suite would produce nine more tasks silently scored 0.0 each — "reporting zeros as if measured," the exact failure the ticket calls out, because a 0.0 for "the assistant did not answer this" and a 0.0 for "the model is not running" look identical in a mean. If the whole-run abort were used for `InferenceFailed` instead, one timeout on one run would discard the other five runs already measured, defeating the entire worst-of-3 design (`docs/build-plan.md`: "a single malformed output fails an entire turn and errors compound" is the reason worst-case is reported, not a reason to throw the rest away). Also decided: `RUNS_PER_TASK = 3` is a module constant with no CLI flag, rather than a `--runs` option defaulting to 3 — a flag is a way to accidentally (or not) run once and report as three, and the acceptance criteria forbid that outcome outright, not just discourage it.

**Consequences:** a suite with one persistently-slow task cannot get a partial result for the rest of the suite by racing past a dead model — the whole run stops, which is intended (a suite that could not be measured must say so, not report what it managed). Widening this later to "abort only the current task's remaining runs on `InferenceUnavailable`" would need a documented reason, since it re-opens the exact ambiguity this decision resolves.

**Refs:** `AGENTS.md` §9 ticket `M2-EVAL-TEST-063`; `eval/runner.py`; `docs/build-plan.md` quality-gate section; issue #236.

## 2026-08-28 — The library deletes sources, not documents; deleted sources stay in the coverage snapshot

**Decision:** `M2-DELETE-FE-062`'s library control calls `DELETE /sources/{id}`, not `DELETE /documents/{id}` — the library is grouped by source (`docs/ux/library.md` §2: "the source *is* the row"), so a row's own delete action tombstones everything under it. `askwell.ingest.coverage`'s snapshot query, which the library's `/ingest`/`/ingest/stream` read, was changed to stop filtering `s.status <> 'deleted'` — a deleted source now stays in the list it always did, with the same row, greyed by the frontend and hidden by a `showDeleted` filter that defaults to off. A new `sources.deleted_at` column (migration `5f3a7c1e9d42`) gives that row a date to show, matching `documents.deleted_at`.

**Why:** `docs/ux/library.md` §4's confirmation copy example ("Delete **supplier-agreement-2024.pdf**?") names a file, which reads as document-level deletion — but §2's own shape statement is unambiguous that collections were removed and the source is the unit the library shows, and §5's state table lists `deleted` as one of the four source statuses a row can be in, not a document-level concept the library surfaces at all. Building a document-level delete control the library never shows anywhere was rejected as scope the ticket's own screen spec does not ask for. Excluding deleted sources from the coverage snapshot was the right call when it was made (`M2` was still future work and nothing needed to render a deleted row), but this ticket is the thing that needed it, and re-deriving "deleted sources exist" from a second query would be exactly the kind of second hand-maintained fact `AGENTS.md` §5 warns about — the snapshot already visits every source once per poll, so it is one `WHERE` clause and one selected column, not a new query.

**Consequences:** any future document-level delete surface (there is none today — `delete_document` exists and is tested but nothing in the frontend calls it) will need its own confirmation flow; this ticket's `DeleteControl` only ever asks for a source. Every other reader of `askwell.ingest.coverage`'s sources list now sees deleted rows too and must filter them if it does not want them — `askwell.retrieve` and the ask-scoping queries were already filtering on `sources.status <> 'deleted'`/`documents.deleted_at IS NULL` independently and are unaffected.

**Refs:** `api/src/askwell/ingest.py` (`coverage`), `api/src/askwell/db/migrations/versions/20260828_5f3a7c1e9d42_source_deleted_at.py`, `web/components/library/library-screen.tsx` (`DeleteControl`), `web/lib/library.ts` (`showDeleted`), `docs/ux/library.md` §2/§4/§5.

## 2026-08-28 — Deletion tombstones a document rather than removing its row

**Decision:** `askwell.sources.delete_document`/`delete_source` (`M2-DELETE-BE-061`) clear a document's chunks' `content` and `embedding` and set `deleted_at`/`deleted_reason`, but keep the `documents` and `chunks` rows. A source deletion does the same to every live document under it and deletes its `schema_notes` outright, leaving `memory` untouched.

**Why:** a hard `DELETE` was the obvious alternative and was rejected — an old answer's citation points at a `chunks.id`/`documents.id`, and a foreign key with nothing behind it either breaks the citation or forces every citation reader to tolerate a missing row silently, which is indistinguishable from a bug. Tombstoning keeps the row as the anchor a citation still resolves against ("deleted on `<date>`") while satisfying the actual requirement — the content and its embedding, the only parts retrieval or a citation viewer could surface, are gone. `deleted_at`/`deleted_reason` were chosen over reusing `superseded_by` because a version and a deletion are different facts (`docs/build-plan.md`'s own validation rule): a superseded document has a live replacement to point to, a deleted one does not, and conflating them would make "what replaced this" ambiguous with "why is this gone."

**Consequences:** every query that must not see deleted material filters on `documents.deleted_at IS NULL` explicitly rather than relying on row absence — already true of `askwell.retrieve`'s two candidate queries and `askwell.ingest`'s coverage/dispatch queries. A stage write that lands after a tombstone (chunk/embed mid-flight during a delete) is not blocked by this decision alone; tracked separately, issue #230.

**Refs:** `api/src/askwell/sources.py` (`_tombstone_document`, `delete_document`, `delete_source`), `api/src/askwell/db/migrations/versions/20260827_a8208099ef38_v1_schema.py` (`ck_chunks_cleared_content_has_no_embedding`), issue #230.

## 2026-08-28 — Conflict presentation is a superset prompt at the same single call site, not a second model call or a second prompt module competing for it

**Decision:** `M2-PARTIAL-BE-059`. `askwell.agent.conflict.compose_conflict` loads a new prompt, `prompts/conflicting_sources.v1.md`, that is `partial_answer.v1.md`'s full content plus a conflict-presentation section and a memory-resolution section — not a fragment composed at runtime, and not a separate prompt chosen instead of the partial one by some prior branch. `askwell.ask._run_generation`'s single composition call site now loads this prompt in place of `compose_partial`'s; `askwell.agent.partial.compose_partial` and `partial_answer.v1.md` themselves are untouched and still independently tested, exactly as `askwell.agent.compose.compose` and `answer_composition.v1.md` were left in place, tested but unused at the call site, when `M2-PARTIAL-BE-057` took it over.

**Why:** there is one model call per turn, and it has to decide, in the same pass, what is covered, what conflicts, and what to write — reusing `M2-PARTIAL-BE-057`'s own reasoning almost exactly: a separate Python-side or second-model-call conflict detector ahead of composition would need to independently agree with whatever the composing call concludes about the same passages, which is two judgements that can disagree about the same fact mid-turn, and a second round trip the latency budget (`docs/ux/ask.md` §7) has no room for. Two alternatives to a superset prompt were considered and rejected. First, editing `partial_answer.v1.md` in place to add the conflict sections: rejected because it would silently change `M2-PARTIAL-BE-057`'s already-shipped, already-tested prompt out from under its own tests and its own eval assumptions, for a concern that ticket never scoped. Second, keeping `compose_partial` as the call site and asking it to also detect conflicts by threading a flag through its existing function signature: rejected because the versioned-prompt convention this repository already uses (`AGENTS.md` §4) treats a materially different system prompt as a new versioned file, not a parameter on an old one — and a second, disconnected boolean controlling which paragraph of the same prompt file applies is exactly the kind of prompt-editing-via-code-branch this project's prompt files exist to avoid.

**Consequences:** `compose_partial`/`partial_answer.v1.md` remain valid, callable, and tested, but are no longer reachable from any live request path — the same state `compose`/`answer_composition.v1.md` have been in since the previous ticket. A future ticket that needs to change partial-coverage wording only has one place to look (`conflicting_sources.v1.md`) for what the model is actually being told today, and must remember `partial_answer.v1.md` is historical, not live — same caveat `answer_composition.v1.md` already carries. Reversing this (going back to a bare partial-only call site with no conflict awareness) means pointing `ask.py`'s one call site back at `compose_partial` and nothing else.

**Refs:** `docs/backlog/M2-it-says-when-it-doesnt-know.md` (`M2-PARTIAL-BE-059`), `api/src/askwell/agent/conflict.py`, `api/src/askwell/agent/prompts/conflicting_sources.v1.md`, `api/src/askwell/ask.py`, issue #223 (OCR-quality gap deferred).

---

## 2026-08-28 — Partial-answer composition replaces the ordinary path for every non-abstained turn, rather than sitting behind a detector

**Decision:** `M2-PARTIAL-BE-057`. `askwell.ask._run_generation` now calls `askwell.agent.partial.compose_partial` unconditionally in the branch that already knows a turn is not abstaining, in place of `askwell.agent.compose.compose`. There is no separate Python-side step that decides "this question is compound, route it through the partial-aware prompt" — every non-abstained turn goes through the one prompt, and a question with nothing left uncovered composes and is marked exactly as it would have before this ticket existed.

**Why:** the ticket's own Assumptions line states aspect decomposition is prompt-driven and imperfect — there is no reliable way to tell "two aspects, only one retrieved" apart from "one aspect, retrieved thinly" by inspecting `Candidate` scores in Python, because the two look identical from a flat, unlabelled list of retrieved passages. The only thing that actually knows what the question is asking about, semantically, is the model that is about to answer it. Building a separate detection call ahead of composition was considered and rejected: it is a second model round trip on every turn (latency the ticket's own budget has no room for — `docs/ux/ask.md` §7's first-token target does not get a second exemption for this), and it would still have to agree with whatever the composition call itself concludes about the same question, which is two chances for the two to disagree about the same fact mid-turn. Folding detection into the one composition call — the model decomposes internally and writes what it could not support as a fixed, parseable line — means there is exactly one judgement, not two that must be kept in sync.

This is why the new prompt (`partial_answer.v1.md`) is a distinct file rather than an added paragraph in `answer_composition.v1.md`, matching the ticket's own "distinct composition path with its own prompt handling, not a variation of the normal answer" line literally: `compose`/`answer_composition.v1.md` are untouched and still exist for anything that still wants the plain path, and `compose_partial`/`partial_answer.v1.md` are what `ask.py` now actually calls.

**Consequences:** the full abstain-vs-partial-vs-ordinary decision tree now has a clean structural boundary rather than a heuristic one — `compose_partial` is categorically unreachable for "every aspect uncovered" because `M2-ABSTAIN-RET-053`'s threshold check already routed that case to `askwell.agent.abstain` before composition runs at all, so the two branches cannot blur by construction, which was one of the ticket's own named edge cases. The cost is that `uncovered_aspects` is only as reliable as the model's own adherence to the `Not covered:` convention — the eval suite this ticket's own Testing Notes point at (`M2`, not built yet) is what will actually measure that miss rate, the same relationship `segment_claims`'s citation-marker convention already has to `M1-CITE-TEST-045`.

**A pre-existing gap this surfaced, not fixed here:** verifying this against the real stack (native `llama.cpp`, the real `Qwen3.5-4B-Q4_K_M.gguf`) showed the model's `<think>` reasoning block — never stripped anywhere in the pipeline before `turn.text` is built — can rehearse the `Not covered:` line more than once while drafting, so `split_partial_answer` (correctly, given what it was handed) extracted three duplicate entries for one real gap. This is a defect in how generation output is consumed generally (`segment_claims`'s citation markers have the identical exposure and predate this ticket), not in the decomposition or the split logic themselves, and is out of this ticket's scope to fix. Filed as issue #220.

**Refs:** `api/src/askwell/agent/partial.py`, `api/src/askwell/agent/prompts/partial_answer.v1.md`, `api/src/askwell/ask.py::_run_generation`, `docs/architecture.md` §7.1, issue #220.
